# Standards pass on the detection suite

## Context

The detection suite was reviewed against the coding, testing and architecture standards of `CLAUDE.md`: `evals/cve/detection/` (the acceptance layer's targets, baseline, gate, recording, session, oracle, markdown, costs, measurement, audit, replay, calibration check, layout and route checks, survey, console and command deps; the holdout layer's targets and `holdout_fixtures/`, run, audit, assembly, results, report, markdown, pre-run check, command deps, draw record, calibration dossier and card readiness), the entry points `evals/run_detection.py`, `evals/run_detection_holdout.py` and `evals/detection_preflight.py`, their unit tests (`tests/unit/detection`), `tests/integration/committed/detection`, the integration tests `test_detection_replay.py`, `test_detection_calibration_check.py` and `test_cve_relaunch.py` with their support under `tests/integration/support/`, and the fake `tests/fakes/image_store.py`, 177 files in all. `evals/cve/detection/holdout_environments.py` was left out: the fixture fingerprint hashes it.

A mechanical inventory listed the functions over 20 body lines, over 3 arguments and the files over 300 lines (85 items, 13 files over 300 lines). Nineteen reviewers then read the code, fifteen file by file and four across the whole area (architecture and responsibilities, duplication and missing concepts, ubiquitous language, the test suite as a whole), and reported 164 findings. Eleven more verified each finding against the code, rejected those that did not hold or traded one standard for another, merged the duplicates, deferred what belongs to another area, and designed the fix of the rest. This plan carries the 83 fixes that survived: 30 of medium value and 53 of low value, 71 small and 12 medium in effort. No fix is a high-priority defect. Most of what is left falls in five kinds:
- the helpers the pass on the eval instruments shared in `evals/` and the detection suite still re-implements (exit codes, refusals, `ModelConditions.of`, `write_json_atomically`, `run_count_problems`, `RecordingRef.of`, `print_throttles`);
- infrastructure both suites use that lives in a detection module (`image_tag` and the image-id lookups, `RunOverrides` and `build_auditor`, the launch retry, the report lines of both holdout reports, `RunInterrupted`, the kit files every blind kit shares);
- copies inside the detection suite (`_refuse_tracing`, the precedence of the drop causes, the throttle line, the launch-error printer, the exchange recording of the survey);
- a few modules that mix responsibilities (`layout_check.py`, the detection exporter in `evals/export.py`, `default_deps` in the holdout run's orchestrator);
- names and test support (the bare `budget`, "replay" for a retest in test names, the unqualified rule version, test files whose names or places do not match what they test, givens copied from the shared builders).

## Approach

Each fix below is small and stands on its own, except where the order notes of its section say otherwise. They are grouped in eleven sections, in the order they are implemented:

1. the adoption of the shared helpers of `evals/`, first, since it shrinks the modules later sections reshape;
2. the moves of shared infrastructure out of detection modules (to `evals/cve/images.py`, `evals/cve/launch_audit.py`, `evals/cve/launch_retry.py`, `evals/cve/kit_dossier.py`, `calibration_check_exit.py` and `draw_record_problems.py`), which change the import lines most later fixes edit;
3. the mechanisms both suites share, extracted under `evals/cve/` (`file_baselines.py`, `holdout_report_lines.py`, `run_files.RunInterrupted`, `launch_audit.print_report_incidents`) and adopted on the detection side;
4. the export, console, markdown and costs fixes;
5. the detection holdout run's orchestrator (`default_deps` moved to `holdout_command_deps.py`, `HoldoutRunInputs`, one drop precedence), which section 6 builds on;
6. the detection holdout command;
7. audit and replay, after sections 2, 3 and 5, whose moves `attempt_once` depends on;
8. the calibration check, the layout check (split in three modules) and the route check;
9. the acceptance session and the detection command;
10. the targets and the draw record;
11. the holdout fixtures, last, behind a fingerprint and call check of their own.

Within a section, the fixes are listed in the order the section's notes give. A reference such as "fix 5.2" names a fix of this plan. The notes and risks of a fix were written by its verifier before the sections were ordered, so a note that reads "another section", or that weighs a fix this plan rejects ("a rejected finding"), describes a neighbour, not a step to take: the order of the sections above wins. A rejected finding is never implemented, even when a note discusses how it would interact.

## Decisions

- **Five fixes change text a user or maintainer reads, each with a line in the `[Unreleased]` section of `CHANGELOG.md`**: fix 6.3 (the holdout calibration check titles a fixture-config error "Crashed:", matching its exit 4), fix 8.2 (under full detail, a layout check launch error prints its unwrapped root cause, as the calibration check already does), fix 5.7 (the acceptance markdown's and console's conditions line, both from `render_conditions`, reads "test budget N"), fix 8.7 (the calibration check's console header reads "layout check then route check") and fix 10.11 (the draw record's load-validation messages name "detection suite rule vN"). No exit code, flag, report key or refusal documented in `CLAUDE.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` changes. Two more fixes change bytes no reader acts on, with no CHANGELOG line (maintainer's decision): fix 9.7 (the acceptance report JSON is written by the shared writer, so non-ASCII is written raw, a very small or large float in plain notation and a non-finite float as `null`, every key and finite value unchanged) and fix 9.8 (the order of the services `python -m evals.detection_preflight` prints). Every other fix is a refactoring and keeps behavior.
- **One labeling-log entry covers the plan's changes to the detection suite's scoring code and fixtures**, as the pass on the eval instruments did for theirs: the gate, recording and baseline code moves and is deduplicated (fixes 1.1, 1.3, 3.1), the fixture fingerprint shares its http-endpoint test with the new list of the files it reads (fix 10.13, every value unchanged), the acceptance report is written through the shared report writer (fix 9.7), the five texts of the first decision change, the holdout fixtures change in text only (section 11), nothing the oracle grades moves, no target, place or grammar changes, and no baseline is reset. The entry is written in the commit of fix 1.1 and lists the later fixes it covers. It states that the grammar fingerprint stays `58afe8a1926bf5fbffa78146b6e7e35d5d5d27797332aea1c829a503cc3e33d1` and that the fixture fingerprint of each of the 41 targets (4 acceptance, 37 holdout) is unchanged, their sorted map hashing to `689da547ef98cf466f67be55cdb28b319790d9e21b8ea6df2df85624c6dc420b` (computed as in Verification), and answers the four questions of ADR 016.
- **The mechanisms both suites share are extracted and adopted on the detection side only.** The nuisance copies (the gate, baseline and recording mirrors, the holdout report and markdown lines, `RunInterrupted`, `_print_incidents`, `backend_image_ids`, `nuisance_overrides`, the throttle lines, the prices of an audit) stay until the pass on the nuisance area removes them, and are listed below so they are not lost. Two exceptions change nuisance code beyond its import lines, because two copies could not coexist: fix 7.3 moves `NuisanceAuditor` to `evals/cve/launch_audit.py` as `SessionAuditor` (its fields unchanged), and fix 5.7 renames `HOLDOUT_BUDGET` to `HOLDOUT_TEST_BUDGET` wherever it is read. Fix 3.3's `partial_result_kept` catches both `RunInterrupted` until then.
- **The import rewrites a fix causes outside this area land in the same commit as the fix**, in `evals/cve/`, `evals/cve/nuisance/`, `evals/capture_probe_corpus.py`, `tests/unit/cve`, `tests/unit/census`, `tests/unit/nuisance`, `tests/unit/support`, `tests/integration/`. No module an eval fingerprint hashes imports a name these fixes rename or move: the only one that imports from `evals/` is `evals/cve/nuisance/counter.py`, through `evals.baseline.fingerprint_sources`, which stays. A name moved out of a module that still imports it passes `pyrefly` as an implicit re-export, so every move is checked by an AST scan of its importers (see Verification).
- **The holdout fixtures change only inside one file at a time.** The shared place-reading helpers, the `Exploit` alias and the `layout.PROJECT` sweep the reviewers proposed are rejected: the fixtures are kit-authored text, shipped as reference to admission kits whose closure has no repository-only helper.
- **Fix 10.3 adds a third sense of "scope" to the glossary's "Words with several senses"**, with no log line, since no word is renamed or moved: "…, or the detection suite's scope: a flaw out of it is a census flaw of an acceptance server whose effect the suite cannot grade or whose target keeps no place, listed with its reason. Code: `OUT_OF_SCOPE_FLAWS`, status `out_of_scope`." The maintainer validated this wording on 2026-11-23. Before its commit, a fresh Opus agent that has not seen the plan checks the sentence against `docs/glossary.md` (style, one meaning per term, no clash with the two other senses), as the project does for normative text written outside its drafting skill.
- **Fix 7.5 adds one integration test that runs a real audit through `audit_launch` with a scripted `FakeLLM`** and checks that the recorded calls agree with the report, the replay's precondition. It produces the replay's input through the real flow, the pattern that found a real bug in the auditor's pass, at the price of a script that follows the graph's order of model calls. It scripts no chain (maintainer's decision, 2026-11-23): the agreement it checks needs only the test cases, a blocked one included, and a chain would make the hand-written script longer and more fragile.
- **Fix 8.10 moves the Docker shim of the integration tests to `tests/integration/support/docker_on_the_host.py`**, and updates the `CLAUDE.md` landmine on container names, which names its file.
- **The follow-ups these fixes open elsewhere are left to the passes on those areas**, and listed here so they are not lost. The nuisance area: adopt `evals/cve/file_baselines.py` in its gate modules (fix 3.1), `holdout_report_lines.py` in its report and markdown (fix 3.2), `run_files.RunInterrupted` (fix 3.3, after which `partial_result_kept` narrows to one exception), `launch_audit.print_report_incidents` (fix 3.4), `evals.cve.images.backend_image_ids` in place of its copy (fix 2.1), `RunOverrides.of_holdout` and `overrides.shape(None)` in place of `nuisance_overrides` (fix 2.2), `eval_display.throttled_lines` and `print_throttles` (fix 4.2), `AuditPrices` (fix 4.3), the nuisance gate exporter moved out of `evals/export.py` (fix 4.1, after which the honeypot suite stops loading `evals/cve/`), the shared helpers of `evals/` its gate still re-implements, and the dead defaults and `default_deps` fallback of its holdout run (fix 5.1). The census and planting area: one derivation of a holdout run's model ids with its `require_ready` (the `model_ids` of fix 5.2 is the detection side's), one refusal for a malformed census beside `load_census`, the advisory URL map and the grammar class text out of `evals/run_census.py`, and the `FIXTURES_PATH` that `tests/unit/cve/support/cve_grammar_golden_corpus.py` restates. The shared test support area: the read server named after the replay (`detection_replay_read_server.py`), used by eight integration files, and an `image_prerequisites` helper fix 8.11 would adopt.

## What stays unchanged

- Every module an eval fingerprint hashes (`evals/cve/detection/holdout_environments.py` and `evals/cve/environments.py` among them), the Dockerfiles and stand-in captures, `docs/adr/`, `plans/` and the two suite rules. `ENVIRONMENT_PATHS` and `GRAMMAR_PATHS` keep their files.
- The fixture fingerprint of every acceptance and holdout target and the grammar fingerprint, checked before and after each section. No `Target` field value or builder function name changes. `targets.py` stays the home of the acceptance targets, which both suite rules name.
- The committed data and how it is read: `evals/baselines/detection/`, `evals/detection_places.json`, the draw record, the census, `evals/holdout_conditions.json`, the calibration cards, and the golden files of the route check, the layout check and the grammar, which do not move.
- The instrument's behavior under the detection suite rule and ADRs 024, 028 to 031, 034 and 036: retest and replay rules, struck and unchecked credits, the pre-run check, calibration verdicts, the holdout run's absence of per-case output and its tracing refusal, exit codes, refusal messages and the command-line flags and their defaults, apart from the five texts of Decisions.
- The keys of every written report, baseline and export (`detection_judged_cases.jsonl` stays byte-identical, since `evals/draw_judge_cases.py` reads it), including the serialized `budget` keys fix 5.7 leaves as they are.
- `tests/unit/project/support/code_standards_debt.py`: no entry is added. The newspaper rule, the one given/then pair per test file and the absence of `monkeypatch.setattr` hold on every file this plan writes.

## Edge cases

- A module move can surface a circular import its old place masked: fix 2.2 must land before fix 7.3, or `launch_audit.py` would import `detection/audit.py`, which imports it. The full test run catches a cycle, and the fix is to import from the defining module, never to add a re-export.
- A rename that misses one reference fails `uv run pyrefly check`; a move whose old module keeps importing the name does not, hence the AST scan.
- A moved test keeps its assertions: moving it is not a reason to rewrite it.
- A holdout fixture's exploits and benign call are outside the fixture fingerprint, so section 11 checks them separately: each touched target's exploits and benign call, run against `load_places(DETECTION_PLACES)` through `tests/fakes`' `FakeMCPClient`, send the same calls before and after.
- The detection holdout run writes no per-case output: no fix may add a print or a file that carries a payload, a response or a verdict to the holdout path (fixes 3.4, 7.3 and 8.2 keep the `TYPES_ONLY` detail of the holdout layer).

## Verification

After each fix: the test files it touches with `uv run pytest <files> -n 0`, then the short loop `uv run pytest tests/unit -n auto --maxprocesses 6`, `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run ruff format .` and `uv run pyrefly check`. A fix that touches committed content or an integration test runs `uv run pytest tests/integration/committed -n auto` and those integration files too (`tests/integration/test_detection_replay.py`, `tests/integration/test_detection_calibration_check.py`, and, behind `CVE_RELAUNCH=1` with Docker, `tests/integration/test_cve_relaunch.py` for sections 2 and 7). Each section closes with `uv run pytest -n auto`. `tests/unit/project/test_code_standards.py` passes at every step without new debt.

Before section 1 and after each section, the fingerprints are computed from the repository root and compared with the values of Decisions:

```python
import hashlib, json
from evals.cve.detection.baseline import fixture_fingerprint, grammar_fingerprint
from evals.cve.detection.holdout_targets import HOLDOUT_TARGETS
from evals.cve.detection.targets import ACCEPTANCE_TARGETS
from evals.cve.places import DETECTION_PLACES, load_places

places = load_places(DETECTION_PLACES)
by_target = {t.flaw_id: fixture_fingerprint(t, places) for t in (*ACCEPTANCE_TARGETS, *HOLDOUT_TARGETS)}
print(len(by_target), grammar_fingerprint())
print(hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(by_target, sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest())
```

(run with `PYTHONPATH=. uv run python`; the import paths of the script follow the moves of the plan). After each move of sections 2, 3, 4, 5 (fix 5.1), 7 (fix 7.3) and 8, an AST scan of every Python file under `evals/` and `tests/` lists the `from X import name` lines that still name the old module of a moved name, and finds none.

## Fixes
### 1. Shared helpers of the eval instruments adopted

Verifier's notes: The confirmed fixes are independent and small, and they can land in one adoption commit or in four. (1) fix 1.1 (baseline.py write_baseline and baseline_integrity, recording._ref). (2) fix 1.2 (ModelConditions.of in baseline.run_conditions and holdout_run._assemble_run). (3) fix 1.3 (gate.exit_code deleted, run_detection exit constants, RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE and DIRTY_TREE_REFUSAL in session_recording and run_detection). (4) Test support: fix 1.4 and fix 1.5. (1) and (2) both edit baseline.py but different functions, so there is no conflict. For the nuisance side (the nuisance area): evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_baseline.py (write_baseline, baseline_integrity, its ModelConditions copy at line 59), evals/cve/nuisance/holdout_run.py:291 and gate_session_recording.py:38 (the dirty-tree literal) can adopt the same area-B helpers on their own. D need expose nothing first. Other D batches on the same modules: another section (fix 2.1, a rejected finding, fix 2.1, fix 2.1, fix 2.1) moves image_ids, backend_image_ids, grammar_fingerprint and image_tag out of baseline.py and holdout_run.py, so it edits the same files and the same _assemble_run image-id block. Land this batch first and rebase that one onto it, or the reverse: the edits touch distinct lines. another section (fix 3.1, fix 3.1, fix 3.1) touches gate.py, recording.py and session/session_recording.py. Removing gate.exit_code and recording._ref here simplifies what it moves. another section (a rejected finding _recorded_images, fix 9.6, fix 1.1, a rejected finding, fix 9.1, a rejected finding) touches session.py, session_recording.py, baseline.py and run_detection.py. another section (fix 5.5, fix 5.5, fix 5.2 on _RunContext) and another section (fix 1.4) touch test_detection_holdout_run.py and its given: fix 1.4 and fix 5.5 are the same a_report_missing deletion carried here in fix 1.4, so apply it once. another section touches run_detection_holdout.py, whose REFUSED_EXIT and CRASHED_EXIT stay local per the a rejected finding rejection. another section touches console.py and run_detection.py (the export and _print_outcome). The rejected directory-literal findings leave console.py's text unchanged, so there is no interaction there.

#### Fix 1.1: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` `write_baseline / baseline_integrity` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: detection baseline.write_baseline is a line-for-line copy of evals.baseline.write_json_atomically: mkdir, a .json.tmp file, then os.replace. baseline_integrity copies run_count_problems, including the ADR 023 rule that a confirmation holds twice the claimed runs, and changes only the flaw_id prefix. recording._ref (recording.py:148) copies RecordingRef.of.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` (duplication): write_baseline repeats write_json_atomically line for line (mkdir, .json.tmp, model_dump_json(indent=2), os.replace). baseline_integrity repeats run_count_problems word for word (no run, claimed vs claimed*2 with a confirmation, the same 'its conditions and its confirmation' message, the same ADR 023 comment). The only difference is the f"{flaw_id}: " prefix. The judge baseline already uses both helpers.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` (duplication): This area still writes its own copies of three baseline-file mechanics that evals/baseline.py now shares. (1) `baseline_integrity` (baseline.py:296-307) re-implements `run_count_problems` line for line: no run, `confirms` doubling the claimed runs (ADR 023), the same messages, only prefixed with the flaw id. (2) `write_baseline` (baseline.py:288-293) re-implements `write_json_atomically` (mkdir, `.json.tmp`, `os.replace`). (3) `recording._ref` (recording.py:148) re-implements `RecordingRef.of`.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/recording.py` (duplication): _ref(baseline) rebuilds RecordingRef(commit=..., recorded_at=...). RecordingRef.of already does this for any RecordedBaseline, and DetectionBaseline satisfies that protocol. evals/recording.py and evals/judge/recording.py use RecordingRef.of.

Why it was kept: Checked evals/cve/detection/baseline.py:288-307 against evals/baseline.py. write_baseline repeats write_json_atomically step for step: mkdir, the .json.tmp file, model_dump_json(indent=2), os.replace. The only difference is that it creates the directory instead of path.parent, which is the same directory. baseline_integrity is run_count_problems with a `{flaw_id}: ` prefix: the same empty-runs message, the same ADR 023 doubling and the same 'its conditions and its confirmation' text. recording._ref (recording.py:148, called at lines 85 and 96) rebuilds RecordingRef.of, and DetectionBaseline meets the RecordedBaseline protocol (status, commit, recorded_at). The judge baseline already uses these helpers. This is the same knowledge (the ADR 023 run-count rule, the atomic write), so sharing it is a real gain. baseline.py is not fingerprinted, and none of the three functions changes its signature. The nuisance gate keeps its own copies in evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_baseline.py, which is the nuisance area's work.

Change: In evals/cve/detection/baseline.py: `def write_baseline(directory: Path, baseline: DetectionBaseline) -> None: write_json_atomically(directory / f"{baseline.flaw_id}.json", baseline)`. `def baseline_integrity(baseline: DetectionBaseline) -> list[str]: problems = run_count_problems(len(baseline.runs), baseline.conditions.runs, baseline.confirms)`, then `return [f"{baseline.flaw_id}: {problem}" for problem in problems]`, with the docstring kept. Import write_json_atomically and run_count_problems from evals.baseline. Drop `import os`, now unused. In evals/cve/detection/recording.py, replace `_ref(existing)` with `RecordingRef.of(existing)` at both call sites and delete `_ref`. Callers keep their signatures: session_recording, tests/unit/detection/support/detection_sessions.py and test_detection_session_given.py.

Files: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py`, `evals/cve/detection/recording.py`

Tests: No test changes. test_detection_baseline (integrity, write and load round trip), test_detection_recording and test_detection_session_recording must pass unchanged, and they pin the messages and the confirms/replaces refs. Every message stays byte-identical.

Risks: The baseline files' JSON format and key order are unchanged, because the same model_dump_json(indent=2) is used. No fingerprinted module is touched.

#### Fix 1.2: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` `run_conditions` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: run_conditions resolves the provider, model, judge model and both reasoning levels by hand, and calls resolve_model()/resolve_judge_model() twice. holdout_run._assemble_run (holdout_run.py:304-315) repeats the same five-field resolution. The honeypot session and the judge baseline already spread `**ModelConditions.of(settings).model_dump()`.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` (duplication): run_conditions resolves provider, model, judge model and both reasoning levels by hand. This is the code ModelConditions.of was extracted to hold, and the honeypot session already writes **ModelConditions.of(settings).model_dump(). The copy calls settings.resolve_model() twice.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` (duplication): This area resolves the provider, models and reasoning levels by hand in two places. `run_conditions` (baseline.py:86-90) calls `settings.resolve_model()` and `resolve_judge_model()` twice each to fill provider/model/judge_model/reasoning/judge_reasoning. `holdout_run._assemble_run` repeats it for the holdout result. `ModelConditions.of(settings)` is that exact resolution, which the honeypot suite and the judge eval already use.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (duplication): `_assemble_run` (33 body lines) resolves the provider, model, judge model and both reasoning levels by hand (lines 305 and 311-315), which is exactly `ModelConditions.of(settings)`. The judge baseline and the honeypot session already adopted that helper (`**ModelConditions.of(settings).model_dump()`). The same five-line block is copied in `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py:88`, `evals/cve/nuisance/holdout_run.py:291` and `evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_baseline.py:59`.

Why it was kept: baseline.run_conditions (baseline.py:86-90) resolves the provider, model, judge model and both reasoning levels by hand, calling resolve_model and resolve_judge_model twice each. holdout_run._assemble_run (holdout_run.py:304-315) repeats the same five-field resolution. ModelConditions.of(settings) (evals/model_conditions.py) is exactly this resolution, makes the same calls and raises the same ValueError, and the honeypot session and the judge baseline already spread it. Both detection sites take a real mcp_auditor.config.Settings (_RunContext.settings: Settings). DetectionRunConditions is a pydantic model whose field order is set by its declaration, so passing the five fields as a spread keeps the JSON key order of written baselines and the iteration order of condition_mismatches. DetectionHoldoutRunResult is a dataclass that accepts the same keywords.

Change: baseline.py: `return DetectionRunConditions(runs=runs, budget=budget, tools_filtered=True, **ModelConditions.of(settings).model_dump(), grammar_fingerprint=grammar_fingerprint())`, importing ModelConditions from evals.model_conditions. holdout_run._assemble_run: delete the `settings = ctx.settings` line and the `model, judge_model = ...` line, and pass `**ModelConditions.of(ctx.settings).model_dump()` in place of the provider, model, judge_model, reasoning and judge_reasoning keywords of DetectionHoldoutRunResult(...). Do not make DetectionRunConditions inherit ModelConditions: inherited fields would move ahead of runs and budget and change the key order of new baseline files and of mismatch messages. The optional image-id extraction fix 1.2 suggests belongs to the shared-infra batch (image_ids).

Files: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py`, `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py`

Tests: No test changes. test_detection_baseline / test_run_detection (run conditions), tests/unit/detection/test_detection_holdout_run.py and the holdout result tests pass unchanged, and they check that the recorded provider, model and reasoning values are the same.

Risks: The committed baselines in evals/baselines/detection/ are read and compared as before, because field names, values and declaration order do not change. Neither module is fingerprinted. The nuisance copies (evals/cve/nuisance/holdout_run.py:291, evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_baseline.py:59) are the nuisance area's work.

#### Fix 1.3: `evals/cve/detection/session_recording.py` `recording_tree` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: Refusal texts and exit codes that evals/ shares are re-typed in this area. The `--record-baseline cannot run --no-baseline ...` text appears as a literal in session_recording.py:45 and run_detection.py:205, both equal to `RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE`. `the git tree has tracked modifications: record from a clean tree` (session_recording.py:52) equals `DIRTY_TREE_REFUSAL`. `gate.exit_code` (gate.py:209) re-maps GREEN/RED/NOT_COMPARABLE to 0/1/3 beside `GateOutcome.exit_code`. run_detection.py:70-71 redefines `NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT = 3`, `CRASHED_EXIT = 4`, and run_detection_holdout.py:52-53 redefines `REFUSED_EXIT = 3`, `CRASHED_EXIT = 4`, beside gate_outcome's `NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT` (documented as 'also the exit code of a refusal') and `CRASHED_EXIT`.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/gate.py` (duplication): exit_code(outcome) maps GREEN 0, RED 1, NOT_COMPARABLE 3 in a dict literal. That mapping duplicates the GateOutcome.exit_code property, which is the documented exit-code contract and uses NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT. session.py calls exit_code(gate.outcome), and test_exit_code in test_detection_gate.py pins the copy.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/session.py` (duplication): run_gated computes exit_code(gate.outcome) through evals.cve.detection.gate.exit_code, a private table {GREEN: 0, RED: 1, NOT_COMPARABLE: 3} that restates GateOutcome.exit_code (which also names NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT). session.py is its only production caller; test_detection_gate.test_exit_code is its only other user.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/gate.py` (duplication): gate.exit_code re-maps GreEN/RED/NOT_COMPARABLE to 0/1/3 with a dict literal, a copy of GateOutcome.exit_code. evals/run_detection.py:70-71 redefines NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT = 3 and CRASHED_EXIT = 4, and evals/run_detection_holdout.py:52-53 redefines them again as REFUSED_EXIT = 3 and CRASHED_EXIT = 4. run_honeypot_suite and run_judge_eval already import them from evals.gate_outcome. That makes four copies of one documented exit-code contract (CLAUDE.md: exit 3 not comparable or refused, 4 crash).

Also covers: `evals/run_detection.py` (duplication): run_detection.py defines NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT = 3 and CRASHED_EXIT = 4 itself, while evals/gate_outcome.py already owns both constants (NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT is also documented there as the refusal exit) and run_honeypot_suite.py, run_judge_eval.py and judge/session.py import them from there.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/session_recording.py` (duplication): recording_tree writes its own literal copies of the two shared refusal messages: "--record-baseline cannot run --no-baseline: a baseline records the conditions it gates" (line 45) and "the git tree has tracked modifications: record from a clean tree" (line 52). The same no-baseline literal is written a third time in evals/run_detection.py:_flag_refusal (line 205). The honeypot and judge sessions already import the constants.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/session_recording.py` (duplication): recording_tree spells out the text of RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE (session_recording.py:44-46) and of DIRTY_TREE_REFUSAL (line 52) by hand. run_detection._flag_refusal (run_detection.py:203-206) holds a third copy of the RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE text. The honeypot and judge sessions import both constants.

Also covers: `evals/run_detection.py` (duplication): _flag_refusal writes out '--record-baseline cannot run --no-baseline: a baseline records the conditions it gates' in full. That is exactly evals.eval_session.RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE, which the honeypot and judge sessions already import.

Why it was kept: Verified each copy. gate.exit_code (gate.py:209) maps GREEN/RED/NOT_COMPARABLE to 0/1/3 beside GateOutcome.exit_code, which test_eval_gate_outcome already pins. Its only production caller is session.py:76, and its only other user is test_detection_gate.test_exit_code. run_detection.py:70-71 redefines NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT = 3 and CRASHED_EXIT = 4, while evals/gate_outcome.py owns both: it notes the 3 as the refusal exit too, and run_honeypot_suite and run_judge_eval import both from there. run_detection is a gated instrument, so this is one exit contract. The text of evals.eval_session.RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE is retyped at session_recording.py:44-46 and run_detection.py:204-206. The text of DIRTY_TREE_REFUSAL is retyped at session_recording.py:52. The honeypot and judge sessions import both constants. These are the same messages and the same codes, so adopting them is a real single-source gain. The holdout part of the finding is not taken up, because REFUSED_EXIT in run_detection_holdout.py names a refusal of a command with no gate (see a rejected finding).

Change: evals/cve/detection/gate.py: delete `exit_code`. evals/cve/detection/session.py: drop `exit_code` from the gate import and write `exit_code=0 if gate is None else gate.outcome.exit_code`. evals/run_detection.py: delete the local NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT and CRASHED_EXIT and add `from evals.gate_outcome import CRASHED_EXIT, NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT`. In _flag_refusal, `return RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE`, added to the existing `from evals.eval_session import RECORDING_REFUSED, Refused`. evals/cve/detection/session_recording.py: in recording_tree, `raise Refused(RECORDING_REFUSED, [RECORD_WITHOUT_BASELINE])` and `reasons.append(DIRTY_TREE_REFUSAL)`, with both imported from evals.eval_session. Leave evals/run_detection_holdout.py's REFUSED_EXIT and CRASHED_EXIT as they are. No test imports run_detection.NOT_COMPARABLE_EXIT or CRASHED_EXIT, so nothing else needs repointing.

#### Fix 2.1: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` `image_ids` (hexagonal-boundary, refactoring)

Problem: Docker image identity is split across the wrong modules. baseline.py, whose job is the baseline model and the fixture/grammar fingerprints, also runs `docker image inspect` (`image_ids`, `backend_image_ids`). The nuisance suite imports `image_ids` from it (gate_session.py, holdout_run.py), and evals/cve/nuisance/images.py:22 carries a verbatim copy of `backend_image_ids`. `image_tag` sits in detection/targets.py next to ACCEPTANCE_TARGETS, so the shared evals/cve/images.py ('the identity of the images built from evals/docker/') imports detection.targets, and so do nuisance backend_registry.py and fix_check_command.py.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/targets.py` (hexagonal-boundary): `image_tag` (the `mcp-auditor-cve-<service>:local` tag of a compose service) is defined in the detection suite's target declarations, yet the shared `evals/cve/images.py` (the compose image identity module, used by both suites) imports it as its default `tag`, and `evals/cve/nuisance/backend_registry.py` imports it too. So loading the shared image module, or the nuisance backend registry, loads the whole acceptance table with its exploits, Kubernetes backends and environments.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py` (duplication): Two `docker image inspect --format {{.Id}}` lookups live in the detection baseline module. Nothing about them is detection-specific: the nuisance gate_session and holdout_run import image_ids from here with functools.partial(image_ids, tag=nuisance image_tag). backend_image_ids is copied verbatim, comment included, into evals/cve/nuisance/images.py.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/targets.py` (other): Several pieces of infrastructure that both suites share live in detection modules. image_tag (detection/targets.py) is imported by evals/cve/images.py and evals/cve/run_images.py, so these shared modules depend on the detection layer, and by nuisance/backend_registry.py and nuisance/fix_check_command.py, where it is aliased to `backend_image_tag`. image_ids, backend_image_ids and grammar_fingerprint (detection/baseline.py) are imported by the nuisance gate and holdout run. HOLDOUT_BUDGET (detection/holdout_run.py) and RunOverrides/build_auditor (detection/audit.py) are imported by nuisance/holdout_run.py, gate_baseline.py and gate_session.py. So every nuisance command loads the detection baseline, targets and holdout orchestrator modules to get Docker and run-condition helpers.

Why it was kept: True as stated. `evals/cve/images.py:13` imports `image_tag` from `evals.cve.detection.targets` and uses it as the default `tag` of `ensure_images` and `stale_services`. So the shared image module loads the acceptance table, its exploits and the Kubernetes backends. `nuisance/backend_registry.py:54` and `nuisance/fix_check_command.py:18` (aliased `backend_image_tag`) import it from there too. The `mcp-auditor-cve-<service>:local` tag is the compose default namespace that the nuisance backend images also use, so it is not a detection concept. `image_ids` and `backend_image_ids` (baseline.py:250-277) are `docker image inspect` lookups in the module of the baseline model. The nuisance gate_session.py:16 and holdout_run.py:23 import `image_ids` from it, and `nuisance/images.py:22` holds a verbatim copy of `backend_image_ids`. No fingerprinted module imports any of the three names (I grepped the 45 files), and `fixture_fingerprint` hashes none of them. Moving the two lookups also brings baseline.py from 307 lines to about 280.

Change: Move `image_tag(name) -> str`, `image_ids(names, tag=image_tag)` (check=True) and `backend_image_ids(tags)` (check=False, keeping its comment) unchanged into `evals/cve/images.py`, below `FINGERPRINT_LABEL`/`COMPOSE_FILE`, with no change of name or behavior. images.py stops importing `evals.cve.detection.targets`, and `targets.py` imports `image_tag` from `evals.cve.images` for `Target.environment`. There is no cycle, because images.py imports only docker_paths. Drop `subprocess`, the now unused `Callable`/`Sequence` and the `image_tag` import from baseline.py. Repoint every importer, since a name still imported by targets.py would otherwise pass pyrefly as an implicit re-export. In evals: run_detection.py (`image_ids`), run_detection_holdout.py, detection/baseline.py, detection/holdout_run.py (`image_ids`, `backend_image_ids`, `image_tag`), nuisance/backend_registry.py, nuisance/fix_check_command.py (`from evals.cve.images import image_tag as backend_image_tag`), nuisance/gate/gate_session.py and nuisance/holdout_run.py (`image_ids`), all import lines only. In tests: tests/integration/test_kit.py, tests/integration/test_nuisance_doris_backend.py, tests/integration/support/test_cve_layout_mount_given.py, tests/integration/committed/nuisance/test_nuisance_compose.py, tests/integration/committed/nuisance/test_nuisance_backend_registry_built.py, tests/unit/cve/test_cve_images.py, tests/unit/detection/test_run_detection.py, test_run_detection_holdout.py, test_detection_preflight.py, test_detection_targets.py, tests/unit/detection/support/test_detection_calibration_check_given.py, tests/unit/nuisance/test_nuisance_images.py, tests/unit/nuisance/test_nuisance_backend_registry_built.py and tests/unit/nuisance/fix_checks/test_nuisance_fix_check_command.py. The tests that use `detection_targets.image_tag` switch to `evals.cve.images.image_tag`. Deleting the nuisance copy of `backend_image_ids` in `evals/cve/nuisance/images.py`, and pointing `nuisance_images.backend_image_ids` in gate_session.py and holdout_run.py at `evals.cve.images.backend_image_ids`, is the nuisance area's follow-up once D exposes the shared function.

#### Fix 2.2: `evals/cve/detection/audit.py` `RunOverrides` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: RunOverrides and build_auditor live in evals/cve/detection/audit.py but are imported by four nuisance modules (evals/cve/nuisance/audit.py, holdout_run.py, holdout_run_command.py, gate/gate_session.py), so the nuisance suite loads the detection acceptance audit module for a value object and a factory. The knowledge of how an override becomes an AuditShape is written twice: detection `_audit` (audit.py:209-215) and nuisance `audit_once` (nuisance/audit.py:98-101, `AuditShape(None, overrides.chain_budget, overrides.max_chain_steps, overrides.tool_call_timeout)`). The conversion from HoldoutConditions is also written twice verbatim: `holdout_overrides` (detection/holdout_run.py:225) and `nuisance_overrides` (nuisance/holdout_run.py:205), same three lines and same assert. Detection also threads `RunOverrides | None` and rebuilds a neutral value with `overrides or RunOverrides(None, None, None)`.

Why it was kept: True as stated. `holdout_overrides` (detection/holdout_run.py:225-229) and `nuisance_overrides` (nuisance/holdout_run.py:205-209) are verbatim copies, assert included, of one piece of knowledge: the overrides a holdout run takes from evals/holdout_conditions.json. The conversion of overrides into an AuditShape is written in detection `_audit` (audit.py:209-215) and again in nuisance `audit_once` (nuisance/audit.py:98-101). RunOverrides and build_auditor are imported by four nuisance modules from the detection acceptance audit module. `RunOverrides | None` with its `overrides or RunOverrides(None, None, None)` fallback is an Optional that a value with defaults removes. launch_audit.py is already the module 'shared by the detection suite and the nuisance suite' and holds Auditor and AuditShape. No fingerprinted module is involved.

Change: Move `RunOverrides` and `build_auditor` into evals/cve/launch_audit.py, beside Auditor and AuditShape. Give RunOverrides `None` defaults on chain_budget, max_chain_steps and tool_call_timeout, keeping `audit_every_tool: bool = False`. Add a method `shape(self, tools_filter: frozenset[str] | None) -> AuditShape`, which returns `AuditShape(None if self.audit_every_tool else tools_filter, self.chain_budget, self.max_chain_steps, self.tool_call_timeout)`. Add a classmethod `of_holdout(cls, conditions: HoldoutConditions) -> RunOverrides` holding today's three lines and the assert, with HoldoutConditions imported for typing only, so launch_audit does not load the conditions file module at runtime. In detection, `_audit(launch, target, auditor, overrides)` builds `overrides.shape(target.tools_filter)`. `audit_target`, `attempt_once` and `AuditTargetFactory` (command_deps.py) take `overrides: RunOverrides = RunOverrides()`, and run_detection.py:274 passes `RunOverrides()` in place of None. Delete `holdout_overrides` and use `RunOverrides.of_holdout(conditions)` in holdout_run.py and run_detection_holdout.py:173. Repoint measurement.py and command_deps.py. On the nuisance side, D changes only the import lines (nuisance/audit.py, holdout_run.py, holdout_run_command.py, gate/gate_session.py). The nuisance area then deletes `nuisance_overrides` in favor of `RunOverrides.of_holdout`, and `audit_once` uses `overrides.shape(None)`.

#### Fix 2.3: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` `HoldoutDeps` (missing-concept, refactoring)

Problem: `HoldoutDeps` has 13 fields, including `running_containers` and `remove_containers`, the exact pair that `evals/cve/run_images.ContainerSweep` names, which the acceptance layer's `DetectionCommandDeps.sweep` and the entry point's `RunDocker.sweep` already use. The holdout run re-splits that concept, and its test support wires the two halves of `FakeContainerSweep` by hand (`running_containers=docker.running, remove_containers=docker.remove`).

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (missing-concept): HoldoutDeps carries running_containers, remove_containers and check_networks as loose callables, although ContainerSweep (and RunDocker, with check_networks) already name that grouping and DetectionCommandDeps and HoldoutCommandDeps use them. The 'sweep the targets' containers at the end, even on raise' block, `tags = [image_tag(s) for s in target_services(targets)]` + try/finally `sweep_containers(tags, X.running_containers, X.remove_containers)`, is written three times: run_detection._run (line 126-137), run_detection_holdout._check_calibrations (line 228-235) and holdout_run.run_holdout_targets (line 145-153).

Why it was kept: True as stated. `HoldoutDeps` (holdout_run.py:68-81) carries `running_containers` and `remove_containers` as two loose callables. `evals/cve/run_images.ContainerSweep` names exactly that pair, and the acceptance command (DetectionCommandDeps.sweep) and the holdout command (RunDocker.sweep) already use it. The test support splits a FakeContainerSweep by hand. The context manager proposed by fix 2.3 is not taken. It would add an abstraction over three two-line try/finally blocks. Changing `sweep_containers` to take a ContainerSweep would also reshape the nuisance call sites (containers.py with its ContainerHost protocol, fix_check_command.py), which goes beyond import lines.

Change: Replace the two fields of `HoldoutDeps` with `sweep: ContainerSweep` and keep `check_networks`. `default_deps` passes `DOCKER_SWEEP` in place of `running_containers, remove_containers`, and holdout_run.py drops those two imports. `_refuse_running_containers` reads `deps.sweep.running_containers(tags)`, and the `finally` becomes `sweep_containers(tags, deps.sweep.running_containers, deps.sweep.remove_containers)`, the form run_detection.py:137 and run_detection_holdout.py:235 already use. The signature of `sweep_containers` does not change.

Files: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py`, `tests/unit/support/detection_holdout_runs.py`, `tests/unit/detection/test_detection_holdout_run_containers.py`

Tests: In detection_holdout_runs.deps_auditing, `sweep=docker.sweep` replaces the two keyword arguments. In deps_reaching_nothing, `ContainerSweep(running_containers, remove_containers)` or `FakeContainerSweep().sweep` replaces the two positional callables. test_detection_holdout_run_containers.py:21 becomes `sweep=FakeContainerSweep.running_before_the_rebuild("leftover_filesystem").sweep`. The assertions are unchanged. The nuisance and cve tests that import detection_holdout_runs do not touch these fields, so they are unaffected.

Risks: another section fix 5.1 moves `default_deps` into holdout_command_deps.py, and fix 5.2 reshapes the run's inputs. Do this one-field change first, or inside that move.

#### Fix 2.5: `evals/cve/detection/calibration_check.py` `PRE_RUN_LAUNCH_ATTEMPTS / NotLaunched / first_launch` (missing-concept, refactoring)

Problem: The launch-retry mechanism (PRE_RUN_LAUNCH_ATTEMPTS, the NotLaunched exception and first_launch) lives in calibration_check.py, but it is shared infrastructure: evals/cve/detection/layout_check.py:43 imports NotLaunched and first_launch from it, and evals/cve/nuisance/vetting.py:15 imports PRE_RUN_LAUNCH_ATTEMPTS from it and writes its own loop over it. So the layout check and the nuisance vet depend on the calibration check module only to reach a retry rule (the nuisance suite loads the detection calibration check, with its rich and environments imports, for one constant). The rule 'a launch or handshake that fails is retried, a launch that ran is never retried' is stated in three docstrings and comments (calibration_check.py:35, layout_check.py:305, vetting.py:78).

Why it was kept: The facts hold. layout_check.py:43 imports `NotLaunched` and `first_launch` from calibration_check.py. nuisance/vetting.py:15 imports `PRE_RUN_LAUNCH_ATTEMPTS` from it, so the nuisance vet loads the detection calibration check (rich, grammar, proofs) for one constant. The rule that a failed launch or handshake is retried up to three launches, while a launch that ran is never retried, is infrastructure common to the calibration check, the layout check and the nuisance vet. Today it sits in one consumer. The three docstrings describe each caller's own use, so the duplication claim is weak, but the placement claim stands. The vet keeps its own loop because it returns the last reason, not None, so only the constant is shared there.

Change: Create `evals/cve/launch_retry.py`. Avoid the module name `launch_attempts`, since 'launch attempt' is not among the glossary's senses of 'attempt'. Move `PRE_RUN_LAUNCH_ATTEMPTS` with its rule comment, `NotLaunched` and `first_launch` into it unchanged. calibration_check.py, layout_check.py and nuisance/vetting.py import from it, the last as an import line only. No name changes, and none of the files is fingerprinted.

Files: `evals/cve/launch_retry.py`, `evals/cve/detection/calibration_check.py`, `evals/cve/detection/layout_check.py`, `evals/cve/nuisance/vetting.py`

Tests: No test imports these names (I grepped). Run the short loop, the calibration check integration test and pyrefly. Behavior is the same.

Risks: another section fix 8.2 plans a shared `report_launch_error` 'next to the launch-retry module of the other finding', so this move comes first. The fix 8.5 split of layout_check.py moves the `first_launch` user, so repoint its import wherever `_Observer` lands.

### 3. Mechanisms both suites share, exposed for the nuisance suite

Order and interactions: this section only adds shared functions under `evals/cve/` and adopts them on the detection side. The nuisance copies stay until the pass on the nuisance area removes them (see Decisions).

Verifier's notes: Confirmed fixes of this batch: fix 3.1 (carrying fix 3.1 and fix 3.1), fix 3.2 (carrying fix 3.2 and fix 3.2), fix 3.3 (carrying fix 3.3) and fix 3.4. All four only add shared functions in evals/cve and adopt them on the detection side. None touches a fingerprinted module or a committed record. The nuisance copies are removed by the nuisance area, which then calls the same functions. E's follow-ups: - nuisance/gate/{gate,gate_baseline,gate_recording,gate_session_recording,gate_session,gate_command}.py adopt evals/cve/file_baselines.py. - nuisance/report.py and nuisance/markdown.py drop _two_sides, _contrast_line, _conclusion_line and the markdown copies for evals/cve/holdout_report_lines.py. - nuisance/results.py and nuisance/holdout_run.py delete their RunInterrupted and raise run_files.RunInterrupted. - nuisance/audit.py calls launch_audit.print_report_incidents. The four fixes are independent of one another. Ordering with other batches of this plan: - fix 3.1 shares evals/cve/detection/{gate,recording,recording_console,session_recording,session,baseline}.py with another section: a rejected finding (one directory name and baseline_file_name, consumed by whole_reset and the file-name mapping), fix 1.1 (write_baseline and baseline_integrity, which DETECTION_BASELINE_STORE wraps), fix 1.1 (_ref to RecordingRef.of in recording.py), fix 1.3 / fix 1.3 / fix 1.3 (delete gate.exit_code, deliberately left out of fix 3.1) and fix 1.3 (shared refusal constants in recording_tree). Land another section first, then fix 3.1. another section's a rejected finding (_recorded_images) also edits session_recording.py. - fix 3.2 shares detection/holdout_markdown.py and nuisance/markdown.py with fix 4.2 (another section), which moves the throttled line to evals/eval_display.throttled_lines. Keep throttled_lines out of holdout_report_lines.py. The order is free, but one of the two must rebase on the other's edits. - fix 3.3 changes one import in detection/holdout_run.py, which the another section (fix 5.1, a rejected finding, fix 1.2, fix 5.2, fix 5.1) and the another section (fix 6.1, a finding deferred to another area, fix 6.1) reshape. The order is free. - fix 3.4 edits detection/audit.py and launch_audit.py, which fix 7.3 (another section) restructures (attempt_once, a SessionAuditor in launch_audit.py). Do fix 3.4 before or together with fix 7.3.

#### Fix 3.1: `evals/cve/detection/gate.py` `apply_retests, decide, _named, _describe, condition_mismatches, WHOLE_RESET` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate.py calls itself 'the mirror of evals.cve.detection.gate' and repeats it with renamed enums. apply_retests is identical (INCOMPLETE on a None retest, rule.decide_retests, REGRESSION vs *_NOT_REPRODUCED). The decide skeleton is the same (mismatches plus WHOLE_RESET first, not-comparable names, then RED, then green reasons), and so are _named/_describe ('the miss/alarm reproduced in k of n retests'), condition_mismatches ('{file}: {field} is {recorded!r}, the run has {expected!r}'), the WHOLE_RESET template, _why_not_gated's unconfirmed sentence and exit_code. The ADR 016 and ADR 024 gate rules are written twice.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/recording.py` (duplication): evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_recording.py is 'the mirror of evals.cve.detection.recording' and repeats it almost verbatim. decide_recordings and leaves_gated_set are identical but for types. _confirmation repeats the commit check and the whole ADR 020 refusal text. _replacement and _reset_refusals are the same structure, _comparison_refusals has the same ADR 016 text, and _FAILED_RUN and _ref are the same. recording_console._next_step duplicates nuisance gate_command._next_step ('Commit {dir} by hand once confirmed').

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/session_recording.py` (duplication): load_checked, _recording_drift and most of recording_tree are copied verbatim into evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_session_recording.py (its docstring calls itself "the mirror of evals.cve.detection.session_recording"): same try/except ValueError -> Refused(title, ["<dir> holds an invalid baseline file: ..."]), same integrity-problems refusal, same tree_drift + reload comparison with the message "the baseline files changed during the runs: record again", same condition-mismatch + WHOLE_RESET + dirty-tree pre-check. Only the load_baselines/baseline_integrity/condition_mismatches functions differ.

Why it was kept: True in part. evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate.py, gate_recording.py and gate_session_recording.py all say they mirror the detection modules, and the copies carry the same knowledge: the whole-reset instruction of ADR 020 (WHOLE_RESET, the same text over two directories), the condition-mismatch line format ('{file}: {field} is {recorded!r}, the run has {expected!r}'), the unconfirmed-baseline sentence of _why_not_gated, the ADR 023 confirmation refusal text, the 'never recorded while the gate is red (ADR 016)' text, the all-or-none rule of decide_recordings, leaves_gated_set, _FAILED_RUN, load_checked with its 'holds an invalid baseline file' message, the 'baseline files changed during the runs' drift message, and the recording next-step instruction (recording_console._next_step and nuisance gate_command._next_step, the same words). Rejected parts: apply_retests, decide, _named/_describe and _green_reasons only look alike. The retest rule itself is already shared (evals.gate.RetestRule.decide_retests), and what is left is control flow over two different outcome enums (nuisance has INCONCLUSIVE, detection has UNCHECKED_DECIDES) and different nouns (miss, alarm). A generic skeleton would read worse than the two copies. exit_code is handled by fix 1.3 / fix 1.3 (GateOutcome.exit_code), and _ref by fix 1.1. The name 'unit_gate'/'unit_recording' proposed by the finders is rejected: 'unit' has a glossary sense (what the grammar grades as one). Identifiers avoid the noun 'refusal', which the glossary reserves for provider refusal. D owns the new shared module and the detection adoption. Removing the nuisance copies is E's work.

Change: Add evals/cve/file_baselines.py (the baselines the two CVE benchmark gates keep one file per target or server), pure functions only: - whole_reset(directory: str) -> str (today's WHOLE_RESET text). In detection gate.py, WHOLE_RESET = whole_reset(_BASELINE_DIRECTORY), so its importers do not change. - condition_mismatches_by_file(conditions_by_file: Mapping[str, BaseModel], candidate: BaseModel) -> list[str]. It iterates in the given order and does not sort. detection gate.condition_mismatches stays as a two-line wrapper that passes {_file_name(flaw_id): b.conditions for flaw_id, b in sorted(baselines.items())}, so the order and the text stay the same. - unconfirmed_baseline_detail(commit: str) -> str, used by gate._why_not_gated. - RUN_NOT_COMPLETED (today's _FAILED_RUN). - all_or_none[B](decisions: Mapping[str, B | RecordingRefused]) -> list[B] | RecordingRefused. It replaces recording.decide_recordings, which is deleted. Its body narrows with `not isinstance(d, RecordingRefused)`. - class Gated(Protocol): def gated(self) -> bool; leaves_gated_set(existing: Gated | None, written: Gated) -> bool, moved from recording.py. - refused_confirmation(differences: list[str]) -> RecordingRefused (the ADR 020 text of _confirmation). - red_gate_reason(noun: str) -> str ('the gate found a regression on this {noun}: ...'). - next_recording_step(status: BaselineStatus, command: str, directory: str) -> str. recording_console builds the command from _RECORD_COMMAND and the flags. - @dataclass(frozen=True) class BaselineStore[B]: load: Callable[[Path], dict[str, B]]; integrity: Callable[[B], list[str]]. Also load_checked(directory: Path, title: str, store: BaselineStore[B]) -> dict[str, B], moved from session_recording.load_checked with the same messages, and BASELINE_FILES_CHANGED. In the detection suite, baseline.py declares DETECTION_BASELINE_STORE = BaselineStore(load_baselines, baseline_integrity). session.py and session_recording.py call load_checked(directory, title, DETECTION_BASELINE_STORE). recording.py keeps TargetRecording, decide_recording, _replacement, _reset_refusals, _comparison_refusals (its own _GREEN set and 'miss' sentence) and _baseline_from, and calls the shared texts. gate.py keeps its enums, models, compare_target, apply_retests and decide. Repoint every importer of the moved names, so that no old module re-exports them implicitly: session_recording.py (decide_recordings, leaves_gated_set) and tests/unit/detection/test_detection_recording.py (decide_recordings, renamed all_or_none, and leaves_gated_set). E then deletes its copies in nuisance/gate/gate.py, gate_baseline.py, gate_recording.py, gate_session_recording.py, gate_session.py and gate_command.py, and calls these functions with 'server'/'alarm' and its own directory.

Files: `evals/cve/file_baselines.py`, `evals/cve/detection/gate.py`, `evals/cve/detection/recording.py`, `evals/cve/detection/recording_console.py`, `evals/cve/detection/session_recording.py`, `evals/cve/detection/session.py`, `evals/cve/detection/baseline.py`, `tests/unit/detection/test_detection_recording.py`

Tests: A refactoring: every message stays byte for byte. tests/unit/detection/test_detection_gate.py, test_detection_recording.py, test_detection_session.py, test_detection_session_recording.py and test_run_detection_recording.py must stay green unchanged, apart from test_detection_recording.py importing all_or_none and leaves_gated_set from evals.cve.file_baselines (decide_recordings renamed). The module gets no test file of its own: both suites' gate tests cover it through behavior once E adopts it. The nuisance tests in tests/unit/nuisance/gate/ pin the same texts and stay green when E adopts it. Run uv run pyrefly check (generic BaselineStore and the all_or_none narrowing) and the full short loop.

Message texts are pinned by tests and documented refusals, so keep them exact. The condition-mismatch order must stay as today's (sorted by flaw ID, then mapped to the file name): do not sort inside the shared function, since the nuisance gate sorts by package before its file-name mapping. #### Fix 3.2: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_report.py` `IntervalModel, MethodLine, ConclusionLine, CutoffModel, RunCutoffModel, run_cutoff, _two_sides, _contrast_line, _conclusion_line` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: The detection holdout report module hosts the report infrastructure that both holdout runs share. evals/cve/nuisance/report.py and evals/cve/nuisance/markdown.py import IntervalModel, MethodLine, ConclusionLine, CutoffModel, RunCutoffModel and run_cutoff from evals.cve.detection.holdout_report, so the nuisance suite depends on the detection report module. The nuisance report also copies _two_sides, _contrast_line and _conclusion_line word for word, and so does the field-by-field RunCutoffModel(model=..., date=cutoff.date, type=..., source=..., note=...) construction in both build_report. Both suites' contrast and conclusion already use the shared holdout_statistic types Contrast and Conclusion, so the copies are one mechanism.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_markdown.py` (duplication): evals/cve/nuisance/markdown.py copies the shared lines of the holdout markdown word for word: _span, _run_cutoff_line, _conclusion_text, _controls_section, _throttled_lines, the _INCOMPLETE_RUNS label, and _incomplete_runs_lines (only the attribute that names the touched entry differs: flaw_id or server_package). The first five health facts of _health_facts (non-protocol lines, connection lost, relaunched, not sent, shown cut) are also the same wording in both. Today the label 'Incomplete runs (a model call missed: refused, unreadable or too long)' is written in both renderers, and the tests of each suite pin it separately.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_report.py` (duplication): The report vocabulary both holdout rates share is defined in detection's holdout_report.py: IntervalModel, MethodLine, ConclusionLine, CutoffModel, RunCutoffModel and `run_cutoff`. evals/cve/nuisance/report.py and markdown.py import it from there. The rendering of those models is copied rather than shared. `_span`, `_run_cutoff_line`, `_conclusion_text`, `_controls_section` and `_throttled_lines` in holdout_markdown.py reappear verbatim in evals/cve/nuisance/markdown.py.

The markdown copies (fix 3.2, fix 3.2) are also verbatim: _span, _run_cutoff_line, _conclusion_text, _controls_section, the _INCOMPLETE_RUNS label with _incomplete_runs_lines, and the first five health facts. This is one knowledge, not a look-alike. The rule picks the run cutoff (latest date, generator first on a tie). The two holdout rates are published side by side (glossary: the nuisance rate is published only beside a detection rate), so their interval, cutoff, conclusion and incomplete-run lines must read the same.

#### Fix 3.3: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_results.py` `RunInterrupted` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: RunInterrupted is defined twice, with the same body and the same docstring: here and in evals/cve/nuisance/results.py:172. Only the type annotation of `partial` differs. Because of the two copies, evals/cve/run_files.py has to import both results modules and catch the pair `except (holdout_results.RunInterrupted, nuisance_results.RunInterrupted)`, even though its write_result already takes the union of both run results.

Also covers: `evals/run_detection_holdout.py` (duplication): The guards of a holdout run are written twice inside this area and twice more in the nuisance suite. `_refuse_tracing` is identical in run_detection_holdout.py:238 and holdout_run.py:180, the same refusal title and the same `TRACING_ON; a detection holdout run keeps no trace` reason. The nuisance pair (holdout_run_command.py:120, nuisance/holdout_run.py:189) changes only the run's name. `model_ids = list(dict.fromkeys([settings.resolve_model(), settings.resolve_judge_model()]))` followed by `conditions.require_ready(model_ids)` appears at run_detection_holdout.py:138 and holdout_run.py:136/140, and again in nuisance. The dirty-tree refusal is copied in both orchestrators. `RunInterrupted` is two identical classes (detection holdout_results.py:178, nuisance results.py:172), so evals/cve/run_files.py has to import both suites' result modules to catch them.

Why it was kept: True. RunInterrupted is defined twice with the same body and docstring (evals/cve/detection/holdout_results.py:178 and evals/cve/nuisance/results.py:172). evals/cve/run_files.py.partial_result_kept therefore catches the pair, while its write_result already takes the union of both run results. One exception for 'a holdout run stopped, keep what finished' is one mechanism, and its catcher is run_files.py, which already imports both result types. The alternative location evals/cve/run_interruption.py (fix 3.3) is rejected: it holds suite-neutral async helpers and would gain a dependency on both suites' result types.

Delete the detection copy in holdout_results.py. evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py imports it from evals.cve.run_files (no cycle: run_files imports only the two results modules, which import holdout_conditions and nuisance.exits). Repoint the detection tests tests/unit/detection/test_detection_holdout_run_containers.py, test_detection_holdout_run_interrupted.py and test_run_detection_holdout_run.py. Until E deletes the nuisance copy, partial_result_kept catches (RunInterrupted, nuisance_results.RunInterrupted). E then deletes evals/cve/nuisance/results.py's copy, raises the shared one in nuisance/holdout_run.py, repoints its four tests, and partial_result_kept narrows to `except RunInterrupted`, dropping the holdout_results and nuisance_results module imports.

#### Fix 3.4: `evals/cve/detection/audit.py` `_print_incidents` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: The incident printing and the provider-error split are copied between the detection and nuisance suites. `_print_incidents` (audit.py:191-203) and nuisance `_print_incidents` (nuisance/audit.py:139-148) print the same missed-call line and the same `throttled {n} time(s) by the model provider (HTTP 429)` line, differing only by the label (flaw_id vs server_package). `_provider_error_attempt` (audit.py:139-144) and nuisance `audit_once`'s `except AuditFailed` branch (nuisance/audit.py:105-108) carry the same rule: raise ProviderFatal on a FATAL kind, else describe_provider_error. print_lost_connections, their third sibling, is already shared in launch_audit with a `label: str` parameter.

Why it was kept: True for the incident printing. detection audit._print_incidents and nuisance audit._print_incidents print the same missed-call line and the same 'throttled {n} time(s) by the model provider (HTTP 429)' line, differing only by the label. Their third sibling, print_lost_connections(label, health), is already shared in evals/cve/launch_audit.py, so this follows a precedent the project kept. Rejected part: the provider-error split. Which provider errors are fatal is already decided once, in evals/cve/provider_errors.py (ProviderErrorKind and its classification), so the finder's 'which kinds are fatal' claim is wrong. What remains is a two-line `if failure.kind is FATAL: raise ProviderFatal` inside each audit's own control flow. A helper that raises on one branch and returns on the other would add an indirection for two lines.

Change: Add print_report_incidents(label: str, report: AuditReport, detail: ReportDetail) -> None to evals/cve/launch_audit.py, right after print_lost_connections. It prints, in today's order and wording, each missed model call (escape(detail.missed_call_detail(missed))) and then the throttle line when provider_usage.throttled_requests > 0. In detection audit.py, _print_incidents keeps only the blocked-payload loop and then calls print_report_incidents(target.flaw_id, report, detail), so the output order stays the same (blocked, missed, throttled, then lost connections). E replaces nuisance audit._print_incidents with the same call.

#### Fix 4.1: `evals/export.py` `export_detection_judged_cases` (hexagonal-boundary, refactoring)

Problem: The detection exporter (`export_detection_judged_cases`, `detection_export_path`) lives in the shared evals/export.py. It types its input on `evals.cve.detection.session_model.DetectionAudit`, which imports targets (ACCEPTANCE_TARGETS, backends, environments, planting), replay and layout_check. So `run_honeypot_suite`, which needs only `export_judged_cases`, loads the whole detection suite. The dependency runs from shared evals/ into a suite.

Also covers: `evals/run_detection.py` (other): run_detection imports export_detection_judged_cases from evals/export.py, the honeypot exporter's module, and evals/cve/detection/console.py imports detection_export_path from there too. To type DetectionAudit, evals/export.py imports evals.cve.detection.session_model, so every honeypot or judge run loads the detection suite.

Also covers: `evals/export.py` (other): The detection exporter and detection_export_path live in the shared evals/export.py. Their only users are run_detection.py and detection/console.py. Because export.py imports DetectionAudit from session_model (and GateAudit from the nuisance gate), run_honeypot_suite imports the whole detection and nuisance graph.

Why it was kept: Fact checked. evals/export.py imports DetectionAudit from evals.cve.detection.session_model and GateAudit from evals.cve.nuisance.gate.gate_session_model. `import evals.export` loads 101 evals.cve modules (session_model alone 43, gate_session_model alone 101), and run_honeypot_suite loads 0 evals.cve modules once evals.export is stubbed. So the shared evals/ module depends on both suites, and the honeypot suite loads the CVE benchmark through it alone. The judge eval does not import evals.export (0 cve modules), so that part of the claim is wrong. The only users of export_detection_judged_cases and detection_export_path are evals/run_detection.py:61/238 and evals/cve/detection/console.py:29/52, plus tests/unit/evals/test_export.py. The eval instruments area reshaped export.py (session-export#1, fix 8.3) but left the suite exporters in it. Moving them out is D's work for the detection half, and the change to evals/export.py stays in D. The honeypot suite stops loading the CVE benchmark only once E moves the nuisance gate exporter too, since gate_session_model alone pulls the whole graph.

In evals/export.py, rename `_case_lines` to `case_lines` and `_write_jsonl` to `write_jsonl` (public, for the suite exporters), delete the two detection functions and the `from evals.cve.detection.session_model import DetectionAudit` line. Point the importers to the new module: evals/run_detection.py (`from evals.cve.detection.judged_cases_export import export_detection_judged_cases`), evals/cve/detection/console.py (`detection_export_path`) and the moved test (see Tests). No re-export from evals/export.py. D exposes case_lines and write_jsonl first, and E adopts them.

#### Fix 4.2: `evals/cve/detection/markdown.py` `_render_throttled` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: The line 'Throttled by the model provider: {n} requests at concurrency {c}' and its 'only when n > 0' rule are written six times: evals/cve/detection/markdown.py:_render_throttled, evals/cve/detection/console.py:44-48, evals/cve/detection/holdout_markdown.py:_throttled_lines, evals/cve/nuisance/markdown.py:_throttled_lines, evals/cve/nuisance/gate/gate_report.py:render_throttled and evals/eval_display.py:print_throttles. The plain-text form of that line is also written separately in markdown._render_throttled and holdout_markdown._throttled_lines, and again in the two nuisance markdowns.

Every rendered string stays byte for byte the same.

#### Fix 4.3: `evals/cve/detection/costs.py` `target_costs` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: The billing rule of a run (generator usage at the generator's price plus judge usage at the judge's price) is written three times: costs.target_costs (`call_cost(usage.total.sub(usage.judge), generator_prices) + call_cost(usage.judge, judge_prices)`), evals/cve/detection/holdout_assembly._run_cost and evals/cve/nuisance/assembly.py:44-45. The generator/judge price pair is a CostPricing here and a bare `tuple[Prices, Prices]` unpacked as gen_price, judge_price in holdout_assembly.py:19/29 and nuisance holdout_run.py:279 / assembly.py:34. In costs.py, CostPricing.costs is also a one-line delegation to target_costs, whose only other callers are tests, so the function takes three arguments that the object already groups.

Why it was kept: Fact checked: the billing rule 'generator usage at the generator's price plus judge usage at the judge's' is written in costs.target_costs (`call_cost(usage.total.sub(usage.judge), generator_prices) + call_cost(usage.judge, judge_prices)`), in holdout_assembly._run_cost and inline in nuisance/assembly.py:43-46. AuditReport.generator_usage is provider_usage.sub(judge_usage), the same split. The generator/judge price pair is an unnamed `tuple[Prices, Prices]` built positionally by holdout_prices and nuisance holdout_run._prices, and unpacked as `gen_price, judge_price` in two places. A silent swap is possible, and the pair is a missing Value Object. CostPricing.costs is a one-line delegation to the three-argument target_costs, whose only other callers are tests. The finder's name RolePrices is rejected: the glossary's word-order paragraph already uses 'role' for acceptance/holdout. 'AuditPrices' names the prices one audit is billed at ('audit' keeps its glossary sense).

Change: In evals/pricing.py add `@dataclass(frozen=True) class AuditPrices: generator: Prices; judge: Prices` with `def cost(self, generator_usage: ProviderUsage, judge_usage: ProviderUsage) -> float` returning `call_cost(generator_usage, self.generator) + call_cost(judge_usage, self.judge)`. In evals/cve/detection/costs.py: CostPricing becomes `prices: AuditPrices; as_of: date`, and target_costs is folded into `CostPricing.costs(by_target)` (shared = prices.generator if prices.generator == prices.judge else None, judge=call_cost(usage.judge, prices.judge), total=prices.cost(usage.total.sub(usage.judge), usage.judge)). The docstring's paragraph on the generator and judge split stays as it is: 'role' is no glossary term, and CONTRIBUTING.md states the same split in the same words ('cannot be priced by role'). Delete target_costs. evals/cve/detection/measurement.py: build `CostPricing(AuditPrices(prices[generator], prices[judge]), conditions.prices_as_of)`. evals/cve/detection/holdout_assembly.py: holdout_prices returns AuditPrices, assemble_target takes `prices: AuditPrices` and sums `prices.cost(report.generator_usage, report.judge_usage)`, and _run_cost goes. #### Fix 5.1: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` `run_holdout_targets` (dead-code, refactoring)

Problem: Every caller passes runs, concurrency and deps: the entry point (`run_detection_holdout._audit_drawn_targets`, which passes `deps=_tracked(holdout_deps, ...)`, `runs=args.runs if args.runs is not None else HOLDOUT_RUNS`, `concurrency=args.concurrency`) and the test support (`tests/unit/support/detection_holdout_runs.run`). So the keyword defaults `runs=HOLDOUT_RUNS`, `concurrency=HOLDOUT_CONCURRENCY`, `deps=None` and the fallback `deps = deps or default_deps(places, holdout_overrides(conditions), concurrency)` never run, and they are copied into the `RunHoldoutTargets` Protocol. `HOLDOUT_CONCURRENCY = 6` is read only by these dead defaults, while the CLI hard-codes `--concurrency default=6` and `--runs default=None, help="default 3"` and then resolves None to HOLDOUT_RUNS by hand. The default of the run is written in three places, and the one that applies is not the named constant.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (port-injection): The orchestrator module holds the live wiring of its own dependencies. `default_deps` builds the auditor from `load_settings()`, the image rebuild, the `docker image inspect` lookups, the container sweep, the closed-network check and the clock, so holdout_run.py imports build_auditor, rebuild_images, running_containers, remove_containers and check_closed_network_support. `run_holdout_targets` also falls back on it (`deps = deps or default_deps(places, holdout_overrides(conditions), concurrency)`, line 141). That branch is dead: the command always passes `deps.holdout_deps(...)` (run_detection_holdout.py:173) and every test passes fakes (tests/unit/support/detection_holdout_runs.py:163). holdout_command_deps.py's docstring says it is 'what the run_detection_holdout command reaches outside the process', but the live I/O is built in holdout_run. The acceptance pipeline already splits it: session.run_gated takes a required DetectionHarness, and run_detection._harness builds the live one.

Why it was kept: Checked every caller: run_holdout_targets is called by run_detection_holdout._audit_drawn_targets (passes runs, concurrency, deps) and by tests/unit/support/detection_holdout_runs.run (passes all three); test_run_detection_holdout passes run_holdout_targets as run_targets, reached only through _audit_drawn_targets; the fakes in tests/unit/detection/support/holdout_command_deps.py take *args/**kwargs. So the keyword defaults runs=HOLDOUT_RUNS, concurrency=HOLDOUT_CONCURRENCY, deps=None and the fallback `deps or default_deps(...)` (holdout_run.py:141) never run, and HOLDOUT_CONCURRENCY is read only by them while the CLI hard-codes `--concurrency default=6` and resolves `--runs None` to HOLDOUT_RUNS by hand. The dead fallback is also what keeps the live wiring (default_deps: build_auditor/load_settings, image rebuild, docker inspect, container sweep, closed-network check, clock) inside the orchestrator module (fix 5.1), while the acceptance layer builds its live harness at the command (run_detection._harness) and holdout_command_deps.py already describes itself as what the command reaches outside the process. Once deps is required, default_deps has a single user, holdout_command_deps, so it moves there with no behavior change.

Change: 1) In evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py make `runs`, `concurrency` and `deps` required keyword arguments (no defaults) of run_holdout_targets and of RunHoldoutTargets.__call__ (`deps: HoldoutDeps`, not Optional), and delete the `deps = deps or default_deps(...)` line. 2) Move `default_deps` and the `HoldoutDepsFactory` protocol into evals/cve/detection/holdout_command_deps.py, with the imports they need, from where sections 2 and 3 left them (bounded, check_closed_network_support, RunOverrides/build_auditor from evals.cve.launch_audit (fix 2.2), attempt_once from evals.cve.detection.audit, image_ids/backend_image_ids from evals.cve.images (fix 2.1), DOCKER_SWEEP from evals.cve.run_images (fix 2.3), CheckOptions/check_target, holdout_audit/DroppedRun/IncompleteRun, LayoutCheck/observe_layout, GradedAudit, TYPES_ONLY, Places, rebuild_images, read_tree, SessionThrottles, datetime/UTC, and HOLDOUT_BUDGET + HoldoutDeps from holdout_run). `holdout_overrides` is not moved: fix 2.2 already replaced it with `RunOverrides.of_holdout`. Remove from holdout_run the imports only they used, and repoint holdout_command_deps' own import of `default_deps` and `HoldoutDepsFactory` (holdout_run keeps no re-export: pyrefly would not catch an implicit re-export). Update holdout_run's module docstring (`built from the live settings by holdout_command_deps.default_deps`). HOLDOUT_BUDGET stays in holdout_run (nuisance imports it and _assemble_run records it). 3) In run_detection_holdout._parser: `run.add_argument("--runs", type=positive_int, default=HOLDOUT_RUNS, help=f"default {HOLDOUT_RUNS}, lowerable to 1")` and `run.add_argument("--concurrency", type=positive_int, default=HOLDOUT_CONCURRENCY)` (imported from holdout_run), and pass `runs=args.runs` with no None resolution. The calibration-check subcommand's `--concurrency default=6` is another concept and stays.
