#### Fix 5.2: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` `run_holdout_targets` (argument-count, refactoring)

Problem: `run_holdout_targets` takes 9 parameters (targets, disclosure_dates, conditions, settings, places, census_commit, runs, concurrency, deps), and the `RunHoldoutTargets` Protocol repeats the whole signature, so every parameter change is made twice. Inside, eight of them are immediately packed into `_RunContext` through an 8-argument positional constructor (line 137). The entry point already groups half of them in `_ReadyHoldoutRun(census, conditions, settings)` and then unpacks them again at the call. The nuisance run names the same grouping (`RunPopulation`, `RunProvenance`).

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (naming): `ctx` is the name of the _RunContext built in run_holdout (l.137). It is threaded as a parameter through _run_surviving (l.161), _run_all (l.265) and _assemble_run (l.298), where it is dereferenced about fifteen times (ctx.conditions, ctx.runs, ctx.places, ctx.settings, ctx.disclosure_dates, ctx.census_commit, ctx.concurrency). That is an abbreviation across four functions and more than 150 lines, not a short scope.

Why it was kept: run_holdout_targets takes 9 parameters (two of them ints, runs and concurrency, side by side), the RunHoldoutTargets Protocol repeats the whole signature, and the body immediately packs eight of them into the private _RunContext through an 8-argument positional constructor (holdout_run.py:137) where swapping `runs` and `concurrency` would type-check. That is the project rule's case: parameters accumulate because a concept exists and is already written (as _RunContext) but kept private. Making it the public input removes the positional constructor, the Protocol duplication and the abbreviation `ctx` threaded through four functions (fix 5.2). The nuisance run names comparable groupings (RunPopulation, RunProvenance).

Change: In evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py replace `_RunContext` by a public frozen dataclass `HoldoutRunInputs` with fields `targets: Sequence[Target]`, `disclosure_dates: Mapping[str, date]`, `census_commit: str`, `conditions: HoldoutConditions`, `settings: Settings`, `places: Places`, `runs: int`, `concurrency: int`, and a property `model_ids -> list[str]` (`list(dict.fromkeys([settings.resolve_model(), settings.resolve_judge_model()]))`). Signature becomes `async def run_holdout_targets(inputs: HoldoutRunInputs, *, deps: HoldoutDeps) -> DetectionHoldoutRunResult`, the refusals reading inputs.targets/disclosure_dates/places in the same order, then `inputs.conditions.require_ready(inputs.model_ids)`. RunHoldoutTargets.__call__ becomes `(self, inputs: HoldoutRunInputs, /, *, deps: HoldoutDeps)`. _run_surviving, _run_all and _assemble_run take `inputs: HoldoutRunInputs` (the parameter name `inputs`, never `ctx`). In run_detection_holdout._audit_drawn_targets build `HoldoutRunInputs(targets=deps.drawn, disclosure_dates=..., census_commit=deps.last_commit(args.census), conditions=ready.conditions, settings=ready.settings, places=places, runs=args.runs, concurrency=args.concurrency)` and call `deps.run_targets(inputs, deps=_tracked(holdout_deps, AuditProgress(bar)))`; _ReadyHoldoutRun stays as the entry point's grouping of what it checked. tests/unit/support/detection_holdout_runs.run builds the inputs the same way. No Target field, builder or fingerprint is touched.

Tests: Only tests/unit/support/detection_holdout_runs.run changes (builds HoldoutRunInputs); every test going through it is unchanged. Fakes of run_targets in tests/unit/detection/support/holdout_command_deps.py take *args/**kwargs and stay. Verify with the detection unit tests and pyrefly (Protocol conformance). Fingerprints unaffected (no target module touched).

Interacts with a finding deferred to another area (the model_ids + require_ready pair written in the entry point and the orchestrator): the `model_ids` property gives that fix one place to read; and with another section fix 1.2/fix 1.2/fix 1.2 (ModelConditions.of in _assemble_run), which edit the same function body.

#### Fix 5.3: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_audit.py` `_DROP_PRECEDENCE` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: The precedence of the drop causes (throttled, then provider error, then launch failure) is defined twice in this area, identically: `holdout_audit._DROP_PRECEDENCE` (line 69) reduces the causes of one run's attempts, and `holdout_run._DROP_PRECEDENCE` (line 62) reduces the causes of a target's runs in `_run_target` with a hand-written loop over the same tuple. The nuisance suite carries two more copies (`evals/cve/nuisance/audit.py:172`, `evals/cve/nuisance/holdout_run.py:75`) over its own NotRunCause enum.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (duplication): `_DROP_PRECEDENCE = (NotRunCause.THROTTLED, NotRunCause.PROVIDER_ERROR, NotRunCause.LAUNCH_FAILURE)` is defined identically in holdout_run.py:62, where it reduces a target's runs, and in holdout_audit.py:69, where it reduces one run's attempts. Both apply the one precedence that the holdout_audit docstring states.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_audit.py` (duplication): The precedence of drop causes (throttled, then provider error, then launch failure) is written twice in this area, as identical tuples: holdout_audit.py:69 picks a run's cause from its attempts, and holdout_run.py:62 picks a target's cause from its runs (`_run_target`, line 291). A third copy sits in evals/cve/nuisance/holdout_run.py:75. If the rule's order changes in one place and not the other, a run and its target are dropped under different causes.

Why it was kept: Fact checked: `_DROP_PRECEDENCE = (THROTTLED, PROVIDER_ERROR, LAUNCH_FAILURE)` is written identically at holdout_audit.py:69 (reduces one run's attempts) and holdout_run.py:62 (reduces a target's runs in _run_target with a hand loop). It is one rule (the holdout_audit docstring states it), and the two levels must agree on which NotRunCause the report counts. The nuisance copies (nuisance/audit.py:172, nuisance/holdout_run.py:75) are over the nuisance's own NotRunCause enum (it has BENIGN_CALL_FAILED, not PRE_RUN_CHECK), so they cannot reuse the detection one: E's concern.

Change: In evals/cve/detection/holdout_audit.py add a classmethod on DroppedRun: `@classmethod def from_causes(cls, causes: Collection[NotRunCause]) -> DroppedRun` returning `cls(next(cause for cause in _DROP_PRECEDENCE if cause in causes))`, docstring one line (throttled, else provider error, else launch failure). holdout_audit ends with `return DroppedRun.from_causes(causes)`. In holdout_run._run_target: `if causes: return DroppedRun.from_causes(causes)` then the list of non-dropped outcomes; delete holdout_run._DROP_PRECEDENCE and drop NotRunCause from holdout_run's imports: `_DROP_PRECEDENCE` is its only use there (_run_all reads `outcomes.cause`, never the enum name).

Files: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_audit.py`, `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py`

Tests: Behavior pinned by the existing precedence tests at both levels (test_detection_holdout_audit.py, and the three drop tests of test_detection_holdout_run.py, parametrized by fix 5.6); no new test needed. Full short loop.

Risks: E may later want the same shape on the nuisance DroppedRun; nothing for D to expose since the enums differ.

#### Fix 5.4: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_audit.py` `IncompleteRun` (comments-docstrings, refactoring)

Problem: The docstring says "The report is kept only for its usage and tools count." A maintainer trusting the docstring could strip the report and silently empty that count. Real defect in a living text.

#### Fix 5.6: `tests/unit/detection/test_detection_holdout_run.py` `test_a_target_whose_run_fails_to_launch_is_dropped_for_the_whole_run` (file-length, refactoring)

Problem: The file is 351 lines. Three tests (lines 187-260, 21 to 24 body lines each) build the same setup: a sick and a healthy target, a report, a scripted audit and a run. They differ only in the sick target's three outcomes and in the expected NotRunCause and reason. Together they pin one behavior, the cross-run drop of a target and the precedence of its causes in `_run_target`.

Why it was kept: test_detection_holdout_run.py is 351 lines; the three tests at lines 187-260 build the same sick/healthy setup and differ only in the sick target's three outcomes and the expected NotRunCause and reason. They are one behavior (the cross-run drop and its cause precedence) and read better as one table; the parametrization brings the file to about 300 lines without moving tests out.

Change: Merge the three into `@pytest.mark.parametrize("sick_outcomes, cause, reason", [...])` `test_a_target_with_a_dropped_run_is_dropped_for_the_whole_run`, with three rows: (detected, launch failure, launch failure) -> LAUNCH_FAILURE, "a run failed to launch after retries"; (launch failure, throttled, detected) -> THROTTLED, "throttled by the model provider past every retry"; (launch failure, provider error, detected) -> PROVIDER_ERROR, "a run hit a provider error past every retry". Since the rows need a report for the graded outcome, build rows with a module-level report or a small row factory inside the test. Assert the healthy target alone is assembled and the single not_run entry carries the flaw ID, cause and reason (the literal reasons stay as the spec).

Problem: The area has two names for one concept and leaves the multi-sense word unqualified. run_detection.py:67 names the acceptance default DETECTION_TEST_BUDGET, while holdout_run.py:60 names the same quantity HOLDOUT_BUDGET. In the detection suite the unqualified `budget` sits beside `chain_budget` (RunOverrides, HoldoutConditions) in audit_target, AuditTargetFactory, build_auditor, result_for and run_detection._result, and HOLDOUT_BUDGET names the same quantity the acceptance layer calls DETECTION_TEST_BUDGET. #### Fix 6.1: `evals/run_detection_holdout.py` `_refuse_tracing` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: `_refuse_tracing` is a verbatim copy of `holdout_run._refuse_tracing` (evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py:180): same condition, same title, same reason. The title literal "detection holdout run refused" also appears three times in the command (lines 130, 154, 241) and six times in holdout_run.py. The nuisance holdout run already names its title once (`_REFUSAL`).

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (duplication): `_refuse_tracing` is copied verbatim in `evals/run_detection_holdout.py:238`, with the same title and the same reason `f"{TRACING_ON}; a detection holdout run keeps no trace"`. The refusal title "detection holdout run refused" is written as a literal six times in holdout_run.py (lines 157, 173, 183, 192, 201, 214, 220) and three times in the entry point (lines 130, 154, 241). The nuisance counterpart already names it once (`_REFUSAL = "nuisance holdout run refused"`). Refusal titles are user-facing and documented.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py` (duplication): _refuse_tracing appears verbatim in holdout_run.py:180 and in run_detection_holdout.py:238, with the same title and the same reason. The pair `model_ids = list(dict.fromkeys([settings.resolve_model(), settings.resolve_judge_model()]))` + `conditions.require_ready(model_ids)` also appears in both (run_detection_holdout.py:138-139, holdout_run.py:136-140). The title string "detection holdout run refused" is written nine times across the two modules.

Why it was kept: True. evals/run_detection_holdout.py:238-243 `_refuse_tracing` is a verbatim copy of evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py:180-185: same condition, same title, same reason f"{TRACING_ON}; a detection holdout run keeps no trace". The title literal 'detection holdout run refused' appears 7 times in holdout_run.py (grep -c) and 3 times in the command (lines 130, 154, 241). Both tracing call sites are needed. The command refuses before the 'no holdout target is drawn' exit and before any dep. The orchestrator keeps its own guard, which the detection suite rule and ADR 015 require of any detection holdout run (no per-case output, tracing refused). The guard is the same knowledge, though, and should be written once. No test imports either `_refuse_tracing` or asserts on the title literal. The nuisance side's `_REFUSAL` precedent shows the convention, but its name clashes with the glossary ('refusal: provider refusal only'), so the new constant takes the verb form, as RECORDING_REFUSED does.

Change: In evals/cve/detection/holdout_run.py (the split into a holdout_run_refusals.py is a rejected finding), add `DETECTION_HOLDOUT_RUN_REFUSED = "detection holdout run refused"`. The glossary writes the qualified form when one holdout run is meant. Use it in every `Refused(...)` and `refuse_running_containers(...)` call of holdout_run.py (`_refuse_running_containers`, `_run_surviving`, `_refuse_tracing`, `_refuse_tools_filter`, `_refuse_missing_census`, `_refuse_unknown_places`, `_refuse_dirty_tree`). Rename `_refuse_tracing` to public `refuse_tracing`. In evals/run_detection_holdout.py, import both, delete the local `_refuse_tracing`, call `refuse_tracing()` first in `_run`, and pass DETECTION_HOLDOUT_RUN_REFUSED to `refuse_existing_report` and to the Refused of `_refuse_unready_cards`. The shared model-id derivation that fix 6.1 also points at goes with a finding deferred to another area (deferred).

Problem: The refusal reason hard-codes "not ready under detection suite rule v11", while the threshold it reports is `CARD_CHECK_RULE_VERSION = 11` in evals/cve/detection/holdout_card_readiness.py, which `unready_cards` applies. Raising the constant leaves the headline stating the old version. The module docstring also says 'under rule v11 or later', the unqualified form the glossary forbids, and it restates the constant.

Why it was kept: True. evals/run_detection_holdout.py:155 hard-codes 'drawn targets whose card is not ready under detection suite rule v11', while evals/cve/detection/holdout_card_readiness.py:8 sets CARD_CHECK_RULE_VERSION = 11, which `_check_gaps` applies and already formats into its own gap lines. If the constant goes up, the headline names the wrong version. The module docstring of holdout_card_readiness also writes 'under rule v11 or later', the unqualified 'rule vN' that the glossary rules out ('rule vN: always "detection suite rule v12"...'), and it repeats the constant. Nothing else hard-codes the version: grep finds only the test assertion 'not ready under detection suite rule v11' (tests/unit/detection/test_run_detection_holdout_run.py:49). That assertion stays true at 11.

In holdout_card_readiness.py, reword the module docstring to: "Whether each drawn target's calibration card is ready for a detection holdout run: the card exists, and its card check ran under the detection suite rule version CARD_CHECK_RULE_VERSION names, or a later one." Do not move the Refused into holdout_card_readiness: that module returns gaps and knows nothing of the run's refusal title, and the title comes from fix 6.1.

#### Fix 6.3: `evals/run_detection_holdout.py` `_calibration_check` (ubiquitous-language, message change, CHANGELOG)

Problem: A fixture-config error is printed under the title "Refused: a detection fixture of a holdout target is misconfigured." but exits CRASHED_EXIT (4). The comment above says it is a crash, not a refusal. In the same module, the other exit-4 path prints "Crashed: a fatal model provider error stopped the run." A reader of the output sees "Refused" and expects exit 3.

Why it was kept: A real defect in printed output. evals/run_detection_holdout.py:210-213 prints the title 'Refused: a detection fixture of a holdout target is misconfigured.' and returns CRASHED_EXIT (4). Its own comment calls the error a crash, and CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md and CHANGELOG.md document exit 4 for a fixture-config error. The same module titles its other exit-4 path 'Crashed: a fatal model provider error stopped the run.'. The glossary reserves 'refusal' and says a command 'refuses to start' (exit 3). An operator who reads 'Refused' expects exit 3 and a precondition to fix, not a fixture bug.

Change: Retitle it "Crashed: a detection fixture of a holdout target is misconfigured." and keep the reason list, the exit code and the comment. Add one line under Fixed in the [Unreleased] section of CHANGELOG.md: the holdout calibration check now titles a fixture-config error as a crash, matching its exit 4.

The kit's contract is thus split between two files, and the CLI file carries knowledge (the service list, the committed captures) that is not about argument parsing or exit codes.

#### Fix 6.8: `tests/unit/detection/test_run_detection_holdout.py` `_drawn` (given-then, refactoring)

Problem: The file tests three subcommands with no given. Its calibration-check setup (`_drawn`, `_current_labels`, `_drawn_target_tags` which recomputes the services `_current_labels` computes, `_cannot_close_a_network`) is private helpers in the test file. The `run` tests are split between this file (lines 26-60) and test_run_detection_holdout_run.py for no behavioral reason. Two setups mislead: `deps_with(failures=["the admission was reached"])` (line 105) uses a nuisance term for a check meant never to run, where the sibling tests write `deps_with(raises=True)`. The tools-filter test (line 47) builds `a_drawn_run_ending_in(a_run_result())`, running a whole fake holdout run whose result is then discarded by `run_targets=run_holdout_targets`.

Why it was kept: Mostly true. (1) test_run_detection_holdout.py holds its calibration-check setup as private helpers in the test file: `_drawn` (a real setup abstraction with a docstring), `_current_labels`, `_drawn_target_tags` (which recomputes the tags `_current_labels` keys already carry) and `_cannot_close_a_network`. It has no given, against the project's Given/When/Then standard. (2) Three `run` tests (no drawn target, tracing on, tools filter) sit here, while test_run_detection_holdout_run.py, whose docstring is 'The holdout run command around its orchestrator', holds the rest of the `run` command's spec. (3) The closed-network test (line 105) uses `deps_with(failures=["the admission was reached"])` as a never-reached sentinel. 'Admission' is a nuisance term, and every sibling test writes `deps_with(raises=True)` for the same purpose. (4) The tools-filter test builds `a_drawn_run_ending_in(a_run_result())`, which runs a whole fake holdout run eagerly, and then replaces its run_targets with the real orchestrator. The result is computed and thrown away, and the test's essential setup is hidden. Not taken: a new single-purpose builder `a_drawn_run_through_the_real_orchestrator()`. Splitting the existing builder serves both uses without adding a helper with one caller.

Change: (a) Move test_run_without_a_drawn_target_exits_zero_and_says_so, test_run_refuses_when_tracing_is_on and test_run_refuses_an_acceptance_target_for_its_tools_filter into tests/unit/detection/test_run_detection_holdout_run.py, which grows from about 153 to about 195 lines. (b) In tests/unit/detection/support/holdout_command_deps.py, split `a_drawn_run_ending_in(outcome)` into `a_drawn_run_through(run_targets: RunHoldoutTargets) -> HoldoutCommandDeps`, which carries today's replace (holdout_deps reaching nothing, last_commit 'cafe', write_report). `a_drawn_run_ending_in` builds its orchestrator and returns `a_drawn_run_through(orchestrator)`. The tools-filter test uses `replace(command_deps.a_drawn_run_through(run_holdout_targets), drawn=(command_deps.a_drawn_target(),))`, with no precomputed result. (c) Create tests/unit/detection/support/test_run_detection_holdout_given.py with `a_drawn_command(*flaw_ids, checks, sweep=None)` (today's `_drawn`, docstring kept), `current_labels(*flaw_ids)` and `a_daemon_that_cannot_close_a_network()`. test_the_calibration_check_removes_the_containers_of_its_images_left_running asserts `containers.listed_tags == [list(given.current_labels(FILESYSTEM))]`, and `_drawn_target_tags` is deleted. (d) The closed-network test uses `deps_with(raises=True)`.

#### Fix 7.1: `evals/cve/detection/replay.py` `_run_patched` (comments-docstrings, refactoring)

Problem: The replay's failure and truncation rules are stated three times in replay.py: the module docstring (paragraphs 3-5), the _run_patched docstring (lines 157-164, restating the lost-connection, stopped-answering, failed-launch and provisional-truncation rules) and the except comment (lines 175-181, restating the teardown rule the module docstring already gives). An amendment to the replay rule (as ADR 036 did) must be made in each copy or they disagree.

Why it was kept: True. The module docstring of evals/cve/detection/replay.py (paragraphs 3-4) states the attempt-failure rules (lost connection at a call the vulnerable run answered, stopped answering, failed launch in its three forms) and the provisional truncation. The _run_patched docstring (157-164) restates all of it. The except comment (175-181) restates that a complete attempt survives a raising teardown because a server logs its shutdown to stdout, already in paragraph 3 of the module docstring. ADR 036 already amended these rules once. Three copies can drift, and the module docstring is the designated home of how the result is computed. replay.py is not fingerprinted.

Change: Cut the _run_patched docstring to: `"""One fresh patched launch: its outputs, or why it could not be reached (see the module docstring). Raises `UndecidableLaunch` on a launch whose container name cannot be read, a configuration error."""`. Cut the except comment to the non-obvious why that the module docstring does not give: the records arrive pre-planted by the vulnerable run, so only launch and connection failures of this fresh patched launch reach here. A hand-picked exception set would crash the whole benchmark on a transient infra blip, worse than the unchecked it returns. A connection already lost keeps its cause, since a dead server's teardown raises too. Drop the mention of `attempt_once` and the stdout shutdown sentence (module docstring, paragraph 3). Leave the module docstring and the defensive-truncation comment in _sent_again unchanged.

Files: `evals/cve/detection/replay.py`

Tests: Comments and docstrings only: no test changes. Run ruff and the short loop.

Risks: Removing the `attempt_once` mention also removes a stale-pointer risk once fix 7.3 moves the capture into `_launched_run`.

#### Fix 7.3: `evals/cve/detection/audit.py` `attempt_once` (function-length, refactoring)

Problem: attempt_once has a 36-line body and 5 arguments. It mixes launching and auditing, billing throttles, printing incidents, capturing the RunToCheck across a raising teardown, classifying infra failures, the throttled exit and grading. Four of its arguments (auditor, throttles, places, overrides) are fixed for a whole session and travel together through audit_target and the holdout default_deps closure; the nuisance suite already names the same grouping NuisanceAuditor(auditor, overrides, throttles).

Why it was kept: True on both counts. attempt_once (evals/cve/detection/audit.py:90-136) has about 40 lines of body. It mixes the launch and audit, the throttle billing, the incident prints, the teardown-tolerant capture of RunToCheck (mutable `run`/`health`), the infra-failure print, the throttled exit and the grading tail. It takes 5 arguments. Three of them (auditor, throttles, overrides) are fixed for a whole session and travel together through the two production closures: audit_target in audit.py and holdout_run.default_deps. The same three are already named as one concept on the nuisance side: NuisanceAuditor(auditor, overrides, throttles) in evals/cve/nuisance/audit.py:81, built in nuisance/holdout_run.default_deps and nuisance/gate/gate_session.default_harness. That makes this the same knowledge, not a look-alike. No fingerprinted module is involved: audit.py, launch_audit.py and nuisance/audit.py are not in ENVIRONMENT_PATHS or GRAMMAR_PATHS.

Change: 1) Extract `_launched_run(target: Target, session: SessionAuditor, records: tuple[Place, ...]) -> tuple[RunToCheck, ServerHealth] | None`. It holds the try / `async with target.launched(records)` block: audit, health, throttles.count, _print_incidents, print_lost_connections and the RunToCheck built inside the block. It also holds the `except (LaunchError, PlantingFailed, subprocess.CalledProcessError)` print with its 'run skipped'/'teardown failed' choice. It keeps the assign-inside / return-after pattern and the existing comment on why the run is captured before the launch is left. AuditFailed is not caught there: it propagates to attempt_once, since _audit raises it before any run is captured. 2) Add `_throttled_attempt(target) -> ThrottledAttempt`, the mirror of `_provider_error_attempt`, which prints the same 'run skipped: throttled by the model provider past every retry' line. 3) attempt_once becomes: records = kept_records(...); try: launched = await _launched_run(...) except AuditFailed as failure: return _provider_error_attempt(...); if launched is None: return None; run, health = launched; if throttled_model_calls(run.report): return _throttled_attempt(target); return await _graded(run, health). Keep its docstring and the configuration-error comment. 4) Grouping. Move NuisanceAuditor from evals/cve/nuisance/audit.py to evals/cve/launch_audit.py as `SessionAuditor` (frozen dataclass, same fields: auditor: Auditor, overrides: RunOverrides, throttles: SessionThrottles), beside Auditor and AuditShape. That needs RunOverrides to live in launch_audit.py first (another section fix 2.2), else launch_audit would import detection.audit, which imports it: a cycle. attempt_once becomes `attempt_once(target, session: SessionAuditor, places)`, and _audit reads session.auditor / session.overrides (overrides no longer Optional, `session.overrides.shape(target.tools_filter)` as fix 2.2 wrote it). audit_target keeps its public signature as fixes 2.2 and 5.7 left it (test_budget, throttles, places, overrides: RunOverrides = RunOverrides()), so AuditTargetFactory and test_run_detection do not change. It builds `SessionAuditor(build_auditor(test_budget), overrides, throttles)`. holdout_command_deps.default_deps (moved there by fix 5.1) builds one SessionAuditor and keeps `throttles` for its `lambda: throttles.requests`. In nuisance/audit.py, nuisance/holdout_run.py, nuisance/gate/gate_session.py and the nuisance test given, only the import line and the type name change (NuisanceAuditor -> SessionAuditor, field names identical), so no second copy of the class exists at any commit. SessionAuditor is a new identifier, absent from docs/glossary.md and docs/glossary-log.md. It follows SessionThrottles, which it carries.

#### Fix 7.5: `tests/integration/support/test_detection_replay_given.py` `_relaunched_at_call_2_run` (test-behavior-vs-implementation, new integration test)

Problem: Every RunToCheck the replay is tested on, in unit and integration, is built by hand: a grammar.a_report(...) report, a recorded_calls list written to match it step for step (_calls, _calls_ending_in_lost_connection), and relaunched_after=[2] set by hand. In production, audit.attempt_once builds the RunToCheck from two independent producers: the graph's AuditReport, read by units_of in tool, case, chain order, and launch_audit's ExchangeLog (exchanges in send order, relaunched_after as the exchange count at each relaunch). arguments_agree requires them to match one to one. No test produces a report and its exchanges through the same audit and checks that they agree (tests/unit/cve/test_cve_exchange_log.py and the replay tests each pin their side of the convention). A divergence would turn every detecting run's credits into unchecked credits: the detection gate would read not comparable, and a detection holdout run would keep them as unchecked.

Why it was kept: The gap is real. In production, attempt_once joins two independent producers: the graph's AuditReport, read by units_of in tool, case, chain order, and launch_audit's ExchangeLog, which holds exchanges in send order and relaunched_after as the exchange count at each relaunch. arguments_agree requires them to match one to one, counting a blocked step as sending no call. Today the graph runs audit_tool then chain_audit_tool per tool (src/mcp_auditor/graph/builder.py:86-98), so they agree. But no test produces both through one audit. tests/unit/cve/test_cve_launch_audit.py uses a fake graph, and every replay test, unit or integration, builds report and recorded_calls by hand. A graph refactor (concurrent tools, chains moved to a later phase) or a change in how the guard records a blocked payload would turn every detecting run's credits into unchecked ones. That would only surface at the next detection gate run (not comparable) or a detection holdout run (unchecked credits kept). There is precedent for a real graph on a FakeLLM against the read server: tests/integration/support/unconfined_audit.a_scripted_audit_of.

Change: Add one integration case to tests/integration/test_detection_replay.py: `test_a_run_recorded_by_a_real_audit_agrees_with_its_report`. In its given (test_detection_replay_given.py), add `async def a_recorded_audit() -> LaunchAudit`. It runs evals.cve.launch_audit.audit_launch on the read server launch (via the docker_on_the_host shim the file already uses), with an Auditor whose llm and judge_llm are a FakeLLM. The FakeLLM is scripted for one tool: a batch of three payloads, one of them destructive so the guard blocks it, their judgments, and the attack context. Script no chain (Decisions), and set the chain budget to 0 so the graph plans none. A blocked payload is never judged, so the script holds one judgment fewer than payloads. Then assert `arguments_agree(units_of(audit.report), audit.exchanges)`, plus `audit.relaunched_after == []`. No replay launch is needed: the assertion is the replay's precondition. The hand-built runs stay for the strike, keep and unchecked paths.

Tests: One new integration test, no Docker daemon, no LLM. Check that it goes red on purpose: temporarily drop a blocked step's exclusion (replay._sent_a_call returning True), then revert. units_of is not the place to sabotage, since evals/cve/report_units.py is in GRAMMAR_PATHS. Run tests/integration/test_detection_replay.py and the full suite.

Risks: The FakeLLM script follows the graph's order of model calls, so a graph change in that order breaks the test. Keep the script as small as the precedent in unconfined_audit.py (cases only, chain optional) to limit that. The read server exposes one tool, so a cross-tool reordering is not caught: say so in the test's docstring rather than add a tool to the shared server (the shared test support area). Apply after fix 7.4 (same given).

#### Fix 8.1: `evals/cve/detection/route_check.py` `RouteObservations` (dead-code, refactoring)

Problem: Two of the six fields of RouteObservations are written and never read. `launch_command` is filled by `route_observations` (line 100) and `_failed_observation` (layout_check.py:328), and nothing in evals/, src/ or tests reads `observations.launch_command`. Its comment claims it is "recorded for the fixture fingerprint", but fixture_fingerprint never sees a RouteObservations (the last reader was removed in the commits after 56cbffe). `tool_names` is only copied into LayoutSurface.tool_names by `_surface` (layout_check.py:349), which nothing reads either: layout_breaks reads tools_list_succeeded and call_errored, nonce_in_surface reads responses, has_route reads scope and responses. Every test literal (test_detection_layout_check.py:22,79, test_detection_route_check.py:92, test_detection_route_check_golden_given.py:23,32) has to fill both fields.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/layout_check.py` (duplication): LayoutSurface repeats four fields of RouteObservations (tool_names, responses, call_errored, tools_list_succeeded), including the same trailing comments. It is only ever built by `_surface` (347-353) and `_surface_pair` (356-357) as a projection of a RouteObservations that every caller already holds. Its `tool_names` is never read. So the module has two types for one observed surface, plus two converters.

Why it was kept: Confirmed by grep. Nothing in evals/, src/ or tests reads RouteObservations.launch_command. It is written in route_observations (route_check.py:100) and _failed_observation (layout_check.py:328), and its comment 'recorded for the fixture fingerprint' is false: baseline.fixture_fingerprint never receives a RouteObservations. tool_names is copied only into LayoutSurface.tool_names, which nothing reads either. LayoutSurface itself (fix 8.1) is a second type for the same observed surface: its only producers are _surface and _surface_pair, which project a RouteObservations every caller already holds. The verdicts (layout_breaks, nonce_in_surface) read fields RouteObservations already has. The golden JSONs record only booleans, so they do not move.

Change: Drop `launch_command` and `tool_names` from RouteObservations (and from route_observations and the failed observation). Make `layout_breaks(reference: RouteObservations, with_layout: RouteObservations)`, `layout_breaks_any(pairs: Sequence[tuple[RouteObservations, RouteObservations]])`, `leaking_places(planted: RouteObservations, nonces)` and `nonce_in_surface(planted: RouteObservations, nonces)` take RouteObservations. Delete LayoutSurface, _surface and _surface_pair. layout_check() calls `layout_breaks_any([(vuln.reference, vuln.planted), (patched.reference, patched.planted)])` and `leaking_places(vuln.planted, vuln.nonces)`. The `launch` parameter of route_observations stays, for launch.scope. This also covers fix 8.1.

#### Fix 8.2: `evals/cve/detection/calibration_check.py` `_report_error` (duplication, output change under full detail, CHANGELOG)

Problem: _report_error repeats layout_check._report_launch_error (evals/cve/detection/layout_check.py:336): both print '<flaw_id> <check> error: <escaped reason>' then rewind the server's stderr and print its last 10 lines through report_detail.server_lines, the tail length 10 being written twice (calibration_check._tail's default and layout_check's literal [-10:]). The two have already drifted: the calibration check unwraps a single-leaf exception group (_root_cause) under full detail, the layout check does not, so the same launch failure reads differently in the two checks of one `--calibration-check` command.

Why it was kept: Confirmed. calibration_check._report_error (234-249) and layout_check._report_launch_error (337-344) print the same format, '<flaw> <check> error: <escaped reason>', then the last 10 stderr lines through report_detail.server_lines, and the 10 is written twice. They have drifted: under FULL_DETAIL the calibration check unwraps a single-leaf exception group (_root_cause) and prints 'Type: cause', while the layout check prints str(exc). Both run inside one --calibration-check command (calibration_check_exit.run_calibration_check), so one launch failure reads two ways. How much server text a holdout-safe log shows is one policy, and it is written twice.

Change: In calibration_check.py, replace `_report_error`, `_tail` and `_root_cause` with one public `report_launch_error(label: str, exc: Exception, server_stderr: IO[str], report_detail: ReportDetail) -> None`. It keeps the calibration check's form: under shows_server_text, unwrap the single-leaf group and print 'Type: cause', otherwise print report_detail.describe(exc), then the 10-line stderr tail through server_lines, with the 10 named once as a private constant (`_STDERR_TAIL_LINES = 10`). `_Exercise.version` calls it with f"{self.target.flaw_id} calibration check error". The layout observer (layout_check._Observer._launch, or layout_observation.py after fix 8.5) calls it with f"{self.target.flaw_id} layout check launch error". Delete `_report_launch_error`. This is a small real defect fix: under FULL_DETAIL a layout check launch error now prints the unwrapped root cause, the same as the calibration check. TYPES_ONLY output (holdout) does not change.

#### Fix 8.3: `evals/cve/detection/survey.py` `_exercise` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: survey._exercise (5 arguments) re-implements what calibration_check._Exercise.version and _Exercise._calls already do: open a TemporaryFile for the server's stderr, enter target.launched(records, version=...) and connect(planted.launch, stderr), run the benign call then each exploit through an ExchangeLog, then grade with PlantedTarget(target.classes, target.aim, planted.nonces). The helpers are copied too: survey._unit is calibration_check._unit verbatim (line 331 vs calibration_check.py:230), survey._call is the body of _Exercise._calls, and the PlantedTarget construction is calibration_check._graded. The survey also hard-codes `connect` where the calibration check takes it through CheckOptions, so its launch path cannot be faked in a unit test the way check_target is. The arguments (target, group, places) are the fields _Exercise already groups.

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/survey.py` (duplication): Survey and the calibration check both record the benign call and each exploit on a connected client through a fresh ExchangeLog: survey._call plus the comprehension in _exercise, and calibration_check._Exercise._calls (line 207). Both then convert the exchanges into a grammar Unit with the same one-liner `unit_of_exchanges([(args, response.content) for _, args, response in exchanges])` (survey._unit line 169, calibration_check._unit line 230). How an exploit's exchanges become a graded unit is the definition the calibration verdicts rest on, and the survey must grade it the same way to choose kept places.

Why it was kept: True in part. survey._unit (survey.py:169) is calibration_check._unit (calibration_check.py:230) word for word. survey._call plus the comprehension in survey._exercise do the same as calibration_check._Exercise._calls: a fresh ExchangeLog for the benign call, then one per exploit. This is shared knowledge: how an exploit's exchanges become one graded unit is what the calibration verdicts rest on, and the survey has to grade the same way to choose kept places. Two parts are rejected. Fusing the whole launch path into one VersionExercise mixes three different error contracts: the calibration check raises NotLaunched before the handshake (so the launch is retried), reports the error and reads the server's stderr; the layout check does the same with another fallback; the survey swallows every exception and retries one launch per place. The calibration check also pings and switches nothing on the benign call, while the survey skips the benign call on the patched version, so a shared object would carry a flag for each caller. Injecting `connect` into the survey brings little: its only test is an integration test with a real read server (tests/integration/test_detection_calibration_check.py:295), and no unit test needs the seam. Sharing the PlantedTarget(...) construction is also rejected: it is one constructor call, written the same way in audit.py, replay.py and kit/. kit/driver.py's own copy of the unit conversion is deliberate, since the kit must stay free of mcp_auditor.

Change: In evals/cve/detection/calibration_check.py, rename `_unit` to public `unit_of(exchanges: list[Exchange]) -> Unit`. Move survey._call there as public `async def logged_exchanges(call: Callable[[Places, MCPClientPort], Awaitable[None]], places: Places, client: MCPClientPort) -> list[Exchange]`, its ExchangeLog named `log`, not the retired `recorder` (this carries the survey half of fix 10.9). Rewrite `_Exercise._calls` as `benign = await logged_exchanges(self.target.benign, self.places, client)` and `exploits = [await logged_exchanges(exploit, self.places, client) for exploit in self.target.exploits]`. In survey.py, delete `_call` and `_unit`, and import `logged_exchanges` and `unit_of` from calibration_check. Keep the survey's benign=False switch on the patched version unchanged (no behavior change). Group survey's `_exercise(target, group, version, places, *, benign)` args into a private frozen `_PlantedGroup(target, group, places)` with `async def surfaced(self, version, *, benign) -> _Surfaced | None`, which `_survey_group` builds once: that brings the call to 2 arguments (CLAUDE.md: aim for 3 arguments or fewer, and name the grouping). Leave the survey's direct `connect` import and its catch-all as they are. This also covers fix 8.3.

Tests: No test changes. tests/integration/test_detection_calibration_check.py (calibration and survey tests) and tests/unit/detection/test_detection_calibration_check.py and test_detection_survey.py must pass unchanged. No fingerprinted module is touched (units.py is left as it is).

Risks: The kit driver (evals/cve/kit/driver.py) states parity with check_target. It keeps its own copy because the kit is mcp_auditor-free, so do not import the new helpers there. The 'recorder' to 'log' rename of fix 10.9 on survey._call is made here, in the moved function.

#### Fix 8.7: `evals/cve/detection/layout_check.py` `module docstring, check_layouts_and_routes console header` (ubiquitous-language, refactoring)

Problem: The layout and route checks go by several living names in the area. layout_check.py:1 says 'The layout break detector and the anti-leak guard', l.12 'The anti-leak guard', and l.149 and l.303 'the break detector'. route_check.py:1 says 'The discovery-route check', and the console header at layout_check.py:186 prints 'layout break then discovery route'. targets.py:45 and :115 say 'The break detector only reports' and '(not the live check)'. The golden givens test_detection_layout_check_golden_given.py:1-11 and test_detection_route_check_golden_given.py:1 use the same names. On top of that, 'the rule gates a fallback launch on a break' (layout_check.py:3), 'the launch the rule gates on a break' (targets.py:44,100), 'no fallback left to gate' (layout_check.py:150) and 'It gates nothing on the model' (l.25) use 'gate' for a check's switch, which the glossary reserves for the baseline comparison. #### Fix 9.1: `evals/cve/detection/session.py` `_retested` (argument-count, refactoring)

Problem: _retested takes four arguments (comparison, target, baseline, harness) and has one caller, _with_retests, whose only work is to look the baseline up in session.baselines() and return early when it is None. The pair splits one decision (retest a fallen target against its baseline) across two functions, and the baseline lookup repeats what _gate already computed.

Why it was kept: True: _with_retests (session.py:186) is a single-caller wrapper whose only work is the baseline lookup and an early return, and _retested (session.py:201) takes four arguments (comparison, target, baseline, harness). The two split one decision. Merging gives one three-argument function of about 20 lines, matching the argument-count and no-single-caller-helper rules. The retest rule (ADR 024) and the UNCHECKED_DECIDES exit (ADR 030) are unchanged.

Change: In evals/cve/detection/session.py replace _with_retests and _retested by one `async def _retested(comparison: TargetComparison, target: Target, session: GatedSession) -> TargetComparison`: `baseline = session.baselines().get(target.flaw_id)`, `if baseline is None: return comparison`, then today's loop unchanged, calling `session.harness.audit(target)` and `apply_retests(comparison, retests, baseline.retest_rule)`, keeping its docstring. In _gate, the gather calls `_retested(c, tr.target, session)`. Delete _with_retests. Keep the function below _gate and _not_run_comparison (newspaper order), with _candidate after it or before as today.

#### Fix 9.4: `tests/unit/detection/support/detection_sessions.py` `ScriptedCheck` (ubiquitous-language, refactoring)

Problem: ScriptedCheck.conditions holds out-of-calibration reasons, while the same module imports and calls conditions() for run conditions (options, a_gated_file, a_baseline_file's `recorded`). The production contract it fakes (CheckTarget) calls them reasons.

Why it was kept: True: ScriptedCheck.conditions (detection_sessions.py:88) holds out-of-calibration reasons, while the same module calls conditions() for run conditions. The glossary lists 'conditions' among words with several senses and asks for the qualified form. The production contract it fakes (CheckTarget) and the glossary-log (`dead_conditions` -> out-of-calibration reasons, `not_run_conditions` -> `not_run_reasons`) call them reasons. Every call site builds ScriptedCheck positionally or by `error=`, so the rename touches two lines.

Change: Rename the field ScriptedCheck.conditions to reasons, and its read in __call__ to `self.reasons.get(target.flaw_id, [])`.

#### Fix 9.5: `tests/unit/detection/test_detection_session.py` `test_a_gated_target_missed_once_is_replayed_until_the_miss_is_decided (and 6 sibling tests)` (ubiquitous-language, refactoring)

Problem: Seven test names in the gate session spec use 'replay' to mean retest and 'settle/settlement' to mean applying retests: test_a_gated_target_missed_once_is_replayed_until_the_miss_is_decided (l.30), test_a_miss_cleared_twice_stops_the_replays_... (l.42), test_no_replay_runs_when_the_conditions_differ_... (l.53), test_a_replay_that_does_not_complete_... (l.65), test_a_settlement_meeting_an_unchecked_replay_ends_unchecked (l.162), test_two_gated_targets_missed_once_settle_their_replays_in_target_order (l.253), test_an_audit_made_by_a_replay_is_not_kept (l.287). The bodies assert on `comparison.retests` and TargetOutcome values. Here the moved meaning causes real confusion: in this suite 'replay' is the ADR 030 resend to the patched version, and these same tests also use unchecked replay credits (an_unchecked_detected_grade). Line 162 mixes both senses in one name: a retest whose audit carries a credit the replay could not check is called 'an unchecked replay', and the application of the retests is called 'a settlement'. The production code (gate.py, session.py) already says retest and apply_retests.

Also covers: `tests/unit/detection/test_detection_session.py` (ubiquitous-language): Six test names of the acceptance session use 'replay' for the retest of a gated miss: is_replayed_until_the_miss_is_decided (l.30), stops_the_replays (l.42), test_no_replay_runs_when_the_conditions_differ (l.53), test_a_replay_that_does_not_complete (l.65), settle_their_replays (l.253), test_an_audit_made_by_a_replay_is_not_kept (l.287). The glossary-log row of 2026-11-11 says this sense moved to 'retest' and that 'replay' now names only the resend to the patched version (ADR 030), which the same file also uses (an_unchecked_detected_grade, l.153). l.162 test_a_settlement_meeting_an_unchecked_replay_ends_unchecked also uses 'settlement', which the glossary reserves for the maintainer's ruling on a disagreement between classifiers or admission agents. No production code of the gate uses either word in this sense.

Why it was kept: True: seven test names in tests/unit/detection/test_detection_session.py (l.30, 42, 53, 65, 162, 253, 287) use 'replay' for the retest of a gated miss and 'settle/settlement' for applying retests. The glossary-log (2026-11-11) says 'replay' in this sense moved to 'retest' and now names only the resend of ADR 030, and that `settle` was renamed `apply_retests` because 'settle' names only a settlement. The confusion is real here: the same file uses the ADR 030 replay (an_unchecked_detected_grade, the comment at l.153). No exemption in retired_word_exemptions.py covers this file.

#### Fix 9.6: `evals/run_detection.py` `_run` (comments-docstrings, refactoring)

Problem: The comment on the --calibration-check branch says the ADR 031 layout and route checks run 'only under the maintainer paths (--calibration-check and --record-baseline), never on a gated comparison'. That is false today: _harness wires layout=observe_layout into every graded session, and session._pre_run_reasons applies layout_failure to every target before any audit, gated runs included (CLAUDE.md: the detection gate runs 'after a pre-run check that runs the calibration check, the layout check and the route check on each target').

Why it was kept: A real defect, a false comment. evals/run_detection.py:129-132 says the ADR 031 layout and route checks run 'only under the maintainer paths (--calibration-check and --record-baseline), never on a gated comparison'. In fact _harness wires `layout=observe_layout` into every graded session (l.280), and session._pre_run_reasons applies layout_failure to every target before any audit, gated runs included, as CLAUDE.md's Commands entry for the detection gate states.

#### Fix 9.7: `evals/run_detection.py` `_write_reports` (duplication, refactoring)

Problem: _write_reports creates the parent directory, writes the report JSON and writes the Markdown beside it with a .md suffix. evals/cve/run_files.write_report(path, report, markdown) already does this for the detection holdout run and the nuisance run. The acceptance command keeps its own copy.

Why it was kept: True: _write_reports (run_detection.py:302) makes the parent directory, writes the report JSON and writes the .md beside it, which is exactly evals/cve/run_files.write_report(path, report, markdown). The detection holdout run, the nuisance holdout run and the nuisance gate already share it. It is the same knowledge (where and how a CVE benchmark report lands). The output differs in the escaping of non-ASCII (model_dump_json writes it raw where json.dumps escapes it), in the notation of very small or large floats (model_dump_json writes `0.000012` where json.dumps writes `1.2e-05`, the same value), and in a non-finite float, which model_dump_json writes as `null`. Its readers (evals/draw_judge_cases.py through evals/judge/draw_checks.py) parse it as JSON, so the values read are the same. run_files imports neither holdout_targets nor anything the acceptance command must avoid (the scan in test_detection_holdout_targets.py checks direct imports, and holdout_results imports only holdout_conditions).

Change: In evals/run_detection.py replace `_write_reports(report, report_path)` with `write_report(report_path, report, render_markdown(report))` imported from evals.cve.run_files. Delete _write_reports and the now unused `import json`. In evals/cve/run_files.py, update the module docstring's 'the report write shared with the nuisance gate' to also name the detection gate's command (docstring only, the census and planting area file).

The only nonce injection in the codebase goes through `Target.planting_spec(..., nonces=...)` directly (one unit test). The parameter is a dead seam that also puts the method over the argument bar.

Why it was kept: True. `Target.launched` (evals/cve/detection/targets.py:136-146) declares `nonces: NonceSource = RANDOM_NONCES`, and no caller passes it: audit.py:105, replay.py:167, survey.py:140, calibration_check.py:193, layout_check.py:313 and tests/integration/support/test_cve_relaunch_given.py:92 pass only `to_plant`, `version` and `shape`. The only nonce injection anywhere is `Target.planting_spec(..., nonces=_FakeNonces())` in tests/unit/detection/test_detection_targets.py:151. So the parameter is a seam nothing uses, and it puts the method over the argument bar. targets.py is not fingerprinted.

#### Fix 10.3: `evals/cve/detection/targets.py` `OutOfScopeFlaw` (ubiquitous-language, glossary entry)

Problem: `OutOfScopeFlaw` / `OUT_OF_SCOPE_FLAWS` use 'scope' in a third sense: outside what the detection suite can grade. In the same file, 'out-of-scope' also means outside the server's filesystem scope (line 269, 'the out-of-scope bare', and line 289, 'git_init(out-of-scope)'), and so does the flaw class `read_outside_scope`. The glossary has no entry for this sense.

Why it was kept: True, but the cheap fix is the right one. `OutOfScopeFlaw`, `OUT_OF_SCOPE_FLAWS`, oracle `TargetStatus.OUT_OF_SCOPE`/`out_of_scope_results` and the serialized status `out_of_scope` use 'scope' for a flaw outside what the detection suite grades. The glossary's 'scope' lists only a target's scope (the checkout) and the root a tool declares. targets.py itself also says 'out-of-scope' in the filesystem sense (lines 269, 289, 388). A rename to UntargetedFlaw would leave the serialized status value `out_of_scope` behind and churn run_detection.py, the oracle and their tests, against the rejected precedent of renaming serialized or widely used names. Documenting the third sense removes the ambiguity.

Change: No code rename. In docs/glossary.md, extend the 'scope' entry under Words with several senses with the third sense, for example: "…, or the detection suite's scope: a flaw out of it is a census flaw of an acceptance server whose effect the suite cannot grade or whose target keeps no place, listed with its reason. Code: `OUT_OF_SCOPE_FLAWS`, status `out_of_scope`." No glossary-log line, since no word is renamed or moved.

Files: `docs/glossary.md`

Tests: None. tests/integration/committed/project/test_glossary.py still passes (no retired word).

Risks: Living doc: memory says rewrites of docs/ go through voix-roman. This is a one-sentence glossary addition, wording to check against the glossary's style.

#### Fix 10.10: `evals/cve/detection/draw_record.py` `draw_problems` (argument-count, refactoring)

Problem: draw_problems takes 5 arguments, and one of them is a vestige: `disclosure_dates: Mapping[str, date]` is only read as `set(disclosure_dates)` (line 155) to know the census identifiers. The dates were read by `_count_problems` (sides per cutoff), removed in 0e5fd77 'print the draw order without sides'. load_census (evals/cve/census/census.py:133) still builds a `{identifier: disclosure_date}` dict for it, so a reader believes the draw is checked against disclosure dates, which it is not.

Why it was kept: True. `draw_problems` (evals/cve/detection/draw_record.py:147) takes five arguments and reads `disclosure_dates` only as `set(disclosure_dates)` (line 155). The dates were read by the per-side count that 0e5fd77 removed ('print the draw order without sides'). load_census (evals/cve/census/census.py:133) still builds `{identifier: disclosure_date}` for it, which misleads a reader into thinking the draw is checked against dates. The only caller is load_census. draw_record.py must not import the census module (its docstring: no cycle), so a Value Object is the way to group what the draw record is checked against.

Change: In evals/cve/detection/draw_record_problems.py (where fix 2.6 moved draw_problems), add `@dataclass(frozen=True) class DrawReferences: census_flaws: frozenset[str]; draw_order_candidates: frozenset[str]; place_ids: frozenset[str]; agents: Sequence[AgentRecord]` ('what the draw record's names must resolve to'; 'draw-order candidate' is the glossary's term, and 'reference' has no conflicting sense with 'draw'). The signature becomes `draw_problems(draw: DrawRecord, references: DrawReferences) -> list[str]`, which passes the fields to the private checks, and drops the `date`/`Mapping` imports it no longer uses. The private checks that take these sets (`_membership_problems`'s `in_census` and `order`, `_walk_problems`'s `order`, `_place_problems`'s `place_ids`) are retyped `frozenset[str]` (or `AbstractSet[str]`), since strict pyrefly refuses a frozenset where a `set[str]` is declared; their bodies only test membership. In census.py, build `DrawReferences(census_flaws=frozenset(flaw.identifier for flaw in census.flaws), draw_order_candidates=frozenset(order), place_ids=frozenset(places.load_places(places.DETECTION_PLACES)), agents=census.agents)` in place of the `place_ids` set and the disclosure-date dict (census.py imports the `places` module, not its names). The messages are identical.

#### Fix 10.11: `evals/cve/detection/draw_record.py` `draw_problems (messages of _walk_problems / _calibration_problems / give-up checks)` (ubiquitous-language, message change, CHANGELOG)

Problem: The draw record's load-validation messages name the rule unqualified in five places: 'walk lists flaw X N times under rule version V' (l.188), '... under rule version V' (l.243), 'flaw X gave up under rule version V, which has no walk step of it' (l.254), 'has two give-ups and a card under rule version V' (l.269) and 'gave up twice under rule version V' (l.272). When the walk step has no version, l.188 prints 'under rule version None'. The sibling module holdout_card_readiness.py:36 and run_detection_holdout.py:155 already write 'detection suite rule v{N}', so the area uses two forms for one concept. The same unqualified form appears in prose at route_check.py:7 ('rule v5'), holdout_card_readiness.py:2 ('rule v11') and tests/integration/committed/detection/test_detection_holdout_targets.py:74 ('Rule v11'). Five load-validation messages of evals/cve/detection/draw_record.py (lines 188, 243, 254, 269, 272) say 'under rule version {version}', and a step with no version prints 'under rule version None', a real defect in the message (the field comments say None means before the first re-walk). route_check.py:7 says 'rule v5' and test_detection_holdout_targets.py:74 says 'Rule v11'. The messages are census load refusals and are not documented in CLAUDE.md or CONTRIBUTING.md.

Leave holdout_card_readiness.py's docstring to fix 6.2, which rewrites it. Add the CHANGELOG line Decisions names, under the `[Unreleased]` section.

### 11. Holdout fixtures

Verifier's notes: Five confirmed fixes, all inside evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/ and all behavior-preserving, except fix 11.3, which corrects false and unqualified comments. None touches a fingerprinted module, a Target field value or a builder name, and exploits are outside fixture_fingerprint. Two fixes touch cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer.py: fix 11.1 (PlaceKind dispatch and `_node_http_get` extraction, with wireshark) and fix 11.2 (dead `__name__` line, with desktop_commander and godot_mcp). Do them in one commit or in sequence. They do not conflict. fix 11.3 (kubectl and github_kanban comments), fix 11.4 (sf_mcp `_call`) and fix 11.5 (claude_in_mobile `HOME` replaced by PROJECT) are independent one-file edits. One verification covers the batch. Before and after, compute fixture_fingerprint for every HOLDOUT_TARGETS entry (must be equal). Then run each touched target's exploits and benign call against load_places(DETECTION_PLACES) with tests/fakes FakeMCPClient and compare the recorded `calls` (must be identical). The touched targets are CVE-2026-55157, CVE-2026-7785, CVE-2025-11489, CVE-2026-25546, CVE-2026-26029 and CVE-2026-81562. No Docker calibration check is needed. All findings about shared helpers (file paths, endpoint URLs, an Exploit alias, deriving commands from Places, PROJECT for /srv/app) are rejected. The holdout fixtures are kit-authored text, shipped as reference to admission kits whose closure lacks repository-only helpers, and drift is governed by the frozen catalogue and the fingerprinted planting code. Other batches: no other batch edits a holdout fixture file. another section a rejected finding (and another section fix 10.1 and fix 10.3, fix 10.9) reshape evals/cve/detection/targets.py. If any of them moves `Target` out of targets.py, every holdout fixture's import line changes, so schedule this batch's fixture edits before that move, or rebase them on it. The changes are disjoint lines.

#### Fix 11.1: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer.py` `_read_command` (function-length, refactoring)

Problem: _read_command (23 body lines) dispatches on `place.kind.value == "file"`, `"env"`, `"hostname"`, `"http_endpoint"`, raw strings instead of the `PlaceKind` enum every other fixture compares with `is PlaceKind.X`. A mistyped kind string would silently fall through to the ValueError and lose that place's exploit. The function also inlines a JavaScript HTTP client assembled by string concatenation and an impossible-case `assert coord.paths is not None` (the loader guarantees path or paths).

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_7785_wireshark.py` (other): `_payload_for` dispatches on `kind = place.kind.value` compared with the bare strings "file", "env", "account", "hostname" and "http_endpoint". The other fixtures of the slice (arcadedb `_command_for`, houtini `_read_path`, mysql `_read_targets`) compare `place.kind is PlaceKind.X`. A misspelled string here type-checks and falls through silently to the `ValueError`, and the dispatch hides the domain enum.

Why it was kept: Verified. cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer._read_command (23 body lines) and cve_2026_7785_wireshark._payload_for both dispatch on `kind = place.kind.value` compared with the bare strings 'file', 'env', 'account', 'hostname' and 'http_endpoint'. These are the only two fixtures that do (grep `kind.value` in holdout_fixtures). Every other fixture compares `place.kind is PlaceKind.X`, and PlaceKind is in places.py, which the kit closure already carries. A misspelled string type-checks and falls through to the ValueError. 55157 also inlines a JavaScript HTTP client built by string concatenation, which pushes the function past 20 lines.

Change: In both fixtures, import PlaceKind from evals.cve.places and compare `place.kind is PlaceKind.FILE / ENV / ACCOUNT / HOSTNAME / HTTP_ENDPOINT`, dropping the `kind = place.kind.value` local. Keep the trailing ValueError message text, with `{place.kind}` in place of `{kind}`. In 55157, extract `_node_http_get(host: str, path: str) -> str`, which returns the `node -e '...'` command, below _read_command, and keep the `assert coord.host is not None and coord.path is not None` at its call. Keep the existing file branch (path, else paths[0], resolve_container_path(raw, WORKING_DIRECTORY)). The shared container_paths helper the finding assumes is rejected (a rejected finding). Every produced payload string stays byte-identical.

Files: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer.py`, `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_7785_wireshark.py`

Tests: No test changes. Verify behavior: before and after, run every exploit and the benign call of CVE-2026-55157 and CVE-2026-7785 against load_places(DETECTION_PLACES) with tests/fakes FakeMCPClient and compare the recorded `calls` lists, which must be identical. Check fixture_fingerprint of every HOLDOUT_TARGETS entry before and after (unchanged: exploits are not hashed, no Target field or builder name moves). Run the full pytest, ruff and pyrefly.

Risks: Holdout fixtures are kit-transplanted text, but in-file refactors are precedented (5c35391, bb97153), and PlaceKind exists in the kit. No fingerprinted module is touched.

#### Fix 11.2: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer.py` `_exploit_for` (dead-code, refactoring)

Problem: `exploit.__name__ = f"exploit_{place_id.replace('-', '_')}"` renames each exploit closure, but nothing reads an exploit's name. The only `__name__` read on a Target field is `target.builder.__name__` in evals/cve/detection/baseline.py:156 (the fixture fingerprint). The calibration check, the survey and the tests never read the names of `exploits`. The same dead line appears in two other slices' fixtures (cve_2026_25546_godot_mcp.py:90, cve_2025_11489_desktop_commander.py:71).

Also covers: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2025_11489_desktop_commander.py` (dead-code): desktop_commander (line 71) and godot_mcp (line 90) rename each exploit closure with `run.__name__ = f"read_{...}"` / `f"exploit_{...}"`. Nothing reads an exploit's __name__. The calibration check labels exploits by index (calibration_check.py:264 `f"{flaw_id} exploit {index}"`), and survey, the kit driver and the fingerprint (which excludes exploits) never look at the name.

Why it was kept: Verified dead. Three fixtures set an exploit closure's `__name__`: cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer.py:72, cve_2025_11489_desktop_commander.py:71 and cve_2026_25546_godot_mcp.py:90. A grep of evals/, tests/ and src/ finds no reader of an exploit's `__name__`. The only callable-name read on a Target field is `target.builder.__name__` (baseline.py:156, fingerprint), and the builder is a different field. The calibration check labels exploits by index, the kit driver and the survey read no name, and tracebacks use `__code__.co_name`, not `__name__`. A reader looking for a consumer finds none, and may suspect the name feeds a fingerprint as the builder's does.

Change: Delete the three `run.__name__ = ...` / `exploit.__name__ = ...` lines and return the closure directly, as the other fixtures do.

Files: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_55157_token_optimizer.py`, `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2025_11489_desktop_commander.py`, `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_25546_godot_mcp.py`

Tests: No test changes. Check fixture_fingerprint of every holdout target before and after (unchanged). The recorded-calls comparison of fix 11.1 covers these three targets too.

#### Fix 11.3: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2025_65719_kubectl.py` `TRIGGER` (comments-docstrings, false comments corrected)

Problem: kubectl's TRIGGER comment says 'The benign username (a plain query) carries no `;`'. The benign call has no username: it is process_natural_language('get pods'). The wording is copied from maigret's fixture, whose benign call is a username search. github_kanban (docstring line 22 and comment line 41) calls /work 'the v11 empty tmpfs' / 'the v11 empty working directory', a bare 'v11' that the glossary forbids without its rule. Its _benign comment (lines 106-108) also restates the module docstring's sentence on list_issues and the three-issue fixture.

Why it was kept: Verified false comment. cve_2025_65719_kubectl.py:44 says 'The benign username (a plain query) carries no `;`', but the benign call is process_natural_language with query 'get pods' (module docstring l.13 and _benign). There is no username: the wording comes from maigret. cve_2025_53818_github_kanban.py:22 and :41 call /work 'the v11 empty tmpfs' and 'the v11 empty working directory'. The glossary ('rule vN') requires the qualified form and never 'v11' alone, and /work is today's layout.WORK, not a version-dated thing. These are the only bare rule versions in the holdout fixtures (grep). The _benign comment of github_kanban adds the GraphQL IssueList detail the docstring lacks, so it stays.

Change: kubectl:44: 'The benign query (`get pods`) carries no `;`.' github_kanban:22: '...so the working directory is the empty /work tmpfs outside the layout, while the checkout stays its scope.' github_kanban:41-42: '...so /work (the empty working directory outside the layout) is the working directory.' Keep the _benign comment.

#### Fix 11.4: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_26029_sf_mcp.py` `_call` (dead-code, refactoring)

Problem: `_call(target_org, *, fields="Id", sobject="Account")`: `sobject` is never passed by either caller (the exploit and the benign call both use "Account"). Only `fields` varies.

Why it was kept: Verified. cve_2026_26029_sf_mcp._call(target_org, *, fields='Id', sobject='Account') has two callers, the exploit at l.78 (`_call(payload)`) and _benign at l.107 (`_call('playground', fields='Id,Name')`). Neither passes `sobject`. It is a parameter never passed.

#### Fix 11.5: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_81562_claude_in_mobile.py` `HOME` (naming, refactoring)

Problem: `HOME = "/tmp" # the planted HOME (cve_planting_spec._HOME); $HOME-rooted places land here` is passed as the second argument of `resolve_container_path(raw, HOME)` in `_cat`, but that parameter is the *working directory*, not the home: `$HOME/` and `~/` coordinates are resolved internally against `planting_spec._HOME`, and the launch's real working directory is `PROJECT` (/srv/app, line 156). So the constant's name and comment say one thing and the call does another. The comment also points to a module that no longer exists (`cve_planting_spec`, now `evals/cve/planting_spec.py`). It has no effect today only because every kept file place is absolute or $HOME-rooted (checked against evals/detection_places.json).

Why it was kept: Verified. In cve_2026_81562_claude_in_mobile.py:39, `HOME = "/tmp" # the planted HOME (cve_planting_spec._HOME)...` is passed as the `working_directory` argument of resolve_container_path, which resolves `$HOME/` and `~/` against planting_spec._HOME itself. The constant's name and comment describe something the call does not do. The pointer `cve_planting_spec` names a module that no longer exists (it is evals/cve/planting_spec.py), and the launch's working directory is PROJECT (l.156), already imported. I computed for every kept file place of the target (19 places, all absolute or $HOME-rooted) that resolve_container_path(raw, '/tmp') == resolve_container_path(raw, PROJECT), so the payloads stay identical.

Change: Delete the `HOME` constant and its comment, and call `resolve_container_path(raw, PROJECT)` in `_cat`, which resolves against the launch's real working directory, the one planting_host declares.

Files: `evals/cve/detection/holdout_fixtures/cve_2026_81562_claude_in_mobile.py`

Tests: No test changes. The recorded-calls comparison for CVE-2026-81562 must be identical, and its fixture fingerprint is unchanged (exploits are not hashed, and planting_host keeps working_directory=PROJECT).

## Due diligence record

What the due-diligence pass concluded about the external facts this plan cites or defers, one line each, for the implementation-phase fact check to read. A line records what was concluded once, not what is true now: no pass may treat it as a reason to skip a verification. A human who edits the corresponding fact by hand deletes its line rather than updating it.

- SETTLED: pyrefly (1.5.0, `preset = "strict"`, the project's configuration) accepts a name imported from a module that only imports it, an implicit re-export, so a missed importer of a moved name passes `uv run pyrefly check` and the AST scan of Verification is needed (verified by a local run of the installed pyrefly on a two-module case)
- SETTLED: pydantic v2 (2.14.1 in the lock) serializes a model's fields in declaration order whatever the keyword order of the constructor, and puts inherited fields before the subclass's own, so `**ModelConditions.of(settings).model_dump()` keeps the key order and inheriting `ModelConditions` would not (fix 1.2) (verified by a local run)
- SETTLED: `model_dump_json(indent=2)` differs from `json.dumps(model_dump(mode="json"), indent=2)` in non-ASCII escaping, float notation (`0.000012` against `1.2e-05`) and non-finite floats (`null` against `NaN`), the values of finite floats parsing the same (fix 9.7, corrected in the plan) (verified by a local run of pydantic 2.14.1)
- SETTLED: the PEP 695 generic syntax of fixes 3.1 and 3.2 (`all_or_none[B]`, `class BaselineStore[B]`, `two_sides[S]`) is valid under the project's `requires-python = ">=3.13"` and pyrefly `python-version = "3.13"` (pyproject.toml)
- SETTLED: `docker compose build [OPTIONS] [SERVICE...]` takes the services as a list with no documented ordering semantics, so the reordered output of `evals/detection_preflight.py` that `.github/workflows/detection-calibration-check.yml` passes to it builds the same images (fix 9.8) (verified against docs.docker.com, docker compose build reference)

## Implementation steps

Each step is one commit, made in this order. A step names its fixes: the text of each fix under Fixes (Change, Files, Tests, Risks) is the specification, and a step adds only what the order settles (which fixes share a commit, what an earlier step already did, which checks to run). A fix with a CHANGELOG line or the labeling-log entry has a commit of its own. Commit messages, CHANGELOG lines and the labeling-log entry describe each change in words, never by a fix or step number of this plan.

### Checks every step runs

1. While a test being written or moved is red or in transit: `uv run pytest <its test files> -n 0`.
2. The short loop: `uv run pytest tests/unit -n auto --maxprocesses 6` (with `--lf` on its failures until they pass, then the full short loop again), `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run ruff format .`, `uv run pyrefly check`.
3. When the step touches committed content (`CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/`, a test under `tests/integration/committed/`) or an integration test or its support: `uv run pytest tests/integration/committed -n auto`, plus the integration files the step names.
4. The fingerprint sweep of Verification. It must print `41 58afe8a1926bf5fbffa78146b6e7e35d5d5d27797332aea1c829a503cc3e33d1` and `689da547ef98cf466f67be55cdb28b319790d9e21b8ea6df2df85624c6dc420b`. Its imports stay valid through the whole plan (`fixture_fingerprint` and `grammar_fingerprint` stay in `baseline.py`).
5. A step that moves a name out of a module: the AST scan below, with the step's `MOVED` map, prints nothing.
6. To close the step: `uv run pytest -n auto`, `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run pyrefly check`. `tests/unit/project/test_code_standards.py` passes with no new entry in `tests/unit/project/support/code_standards_debt.py`.

The AST scan, kept outside the repository (the scratchpad) and run from the repository root with `uv run python <path>`. It lists both `from old import name` and `alias.name` where `alias` is the old module:

```python
import ast
import pathlib

MOVED: dict[str, set[str]] = {}  # old module -> names that left it, set per step

for path in sorted([*pathlib.Path("evals").rglob("*.py"), *pathlib.Path("tests").rglob("*.py")]):
    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text())
    aliases = {}
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.module in MOVED:
            for name in node.names:
                if name.name in MOVED[node.module]:
                    print(f"{path}:{node.lineno}: from {node.module} import {name.name}")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            aliases |= {a.asname or a.name: a.name for a in node.names if a.name in MOVED}
        if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.module:
            aliases |= {
                a.asname or a.name: f"{node.module}.{a.name}"
                for a in node.names
                if f"{node.module}.{a.name}" in MOVED
            }
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Attribute) and isinstance(node.value, ast.Name) and node.value.id in aliases:
            if node.attr in MOVED[aliases[node.value.id]]:
                print(f"{path}:{node.lineno}: {aliases[node.value.id]}.{node.attr}")
```

- **Do**: Before any edit, record the calls of the touched targets with the script below (scratchpad, `PYTHONPATH=. uv run python <path> > before.json`). Apply fixes 11.1 (`PlaceKind` comparisons in both fixtures, `_node_http_get` extracted in 55157), 11.2 (the three `__name__` lines deleted), 11.3 (the kubectl and github_kanban comments), 11.4 (`_call` without `sobject`) and 11.5 (`HOME` deleted, `resolve_container_path(raw, PROJECT)`). Record again to `after.json` and `diff` the two: no difference.

```python
import asyncio
import json

from evals.cve.detection.holdout_targets import HOLDOUT_TARGETS
from evals.cve.places import DETECTION_PLACES, load_places
from tests.fakes.mcp_client import FakeMCPClient

TOUCHED = {"CVE-2026-55157", "CVE-2026-7785", "CVE-2025-11489", "CVE-2026-25546", "CVE-2026-26029", "CVE-2026-81562"}


async def main() -> None:
    places = load_places(DETECTION_PLACES)
    recorded = {}
    for target in HOLDOUT_TARGETS:
        if target.flaw_id in TOUCHED:
            for index, call in enumerate((target.benign, *target.exploits)):
                client = FakeMCPClient([])
                try:
                    await call(places, client)
                    outcome = "ok"
                except Exception as error:
                    outcome = type(error).__name__
                recorded[f"{target.flaw_id}#{index}"] = {"outcome": outcome, "calls": client.calls}
    print(json.dumps(recorded, sort_keys=True, indent=1))


asyncio.run(main())
```

- **Test**: No test change. The recorded calls are identical (149 benign calls and exploits, each ending `ok` on the tree this plan starts from).
- **Verify**: checks 2, 3, 4 (no holdout fixture fingerprint moves), 6.
